Creative Director Samples

Six requests, five approaches, two models. GPT-6.1 Sol (OpenAI API, effort high) · Claude Opus 5.5 (Claude Code subagent).

Animate grandmaBakery adEnergy drink, 6 shotsLighthouse short filmSomething cool for instaDad's 60th

Animate grandma

“animate this photo of my grandma so she smiles and waves, like 5 seconds”

Upload: One photo: an elderly woman with short white hair, round glasses and a green cardigan, sitting in an armchair in a sunlit living room, looking at the camera with a neutral expression.

1. One-shot script

GPT-6.1 Sol

5-second animation script — single, fixed shot

0–1 sec: Grandma’s neutral expression gently turns into a warm smile, with a natural blink and a slight lift of her cheeks.

1–4 sec: Still looking at the camera, she raises one hand and gives a small, relaxed wave. Her smile stays soft and affectionate.

4–5 sec: She begins lowering her hand, holding her smile as the clip ends.

Animation notes: Preserve her facial features, short white hair, round glasses, green cardigan, and seated position. Keep the sunlit living room unchanged. Use subtle, lifelike movement—no camera motion, dialogue, or exaggerated gestures.

Claude Opus 5.5

Grandma Smiles and Waves — 5-Second Photo Animation

Source: Your photo (grandma in the armchair, green cardigan, sunlit living room) Length: 5 seconds Aspect ratio: Match the original photo Camera: Locked off. No zoom or pan, so it feels like the photo itself came to life. Audio: None by default (optional: soft room tone or a gentle piano note)

0.0s – 1.0s — The photo breathes

Grandma sits exactly as in the photo, looking at the camera with her neutral expression. Subtle signs of life only: a slow breath lifts her shoulders slightly, and she blinks once. Sunlight in the room stays still and warm.

1.0s – 2.2s — The smile

Her eyes soften first. The corners of her eyes crinkle behind her round glasses, then a warm, closed-mouth smile spreads across her face. Her head tilts very slightly, as if she just recognized someone she loves.

2.2s – 4.2s — The wave

She lifts her right hand from the arm of the chair to about shoulder height, palm facing the camera, and gives two or three slow, gentle waves. The movement is relaxed and natural for her age, not quick or bouncy. The sleeve of her green cardigan moves with her arm. Her smile grows a little wider, with a hint of teeth.

4.2s – 5.0s — Settle

Her hand lowers back toward the armrest. She holds the smile and keeps looking into the camera as the clip ends.

Animation prompt (for the image-to-video model)
The elderly woman in the photo comes to life. She breathes gently and blinks, then her eyes crinkle and she breaks into a warm, loving smile. She slowly raises her right hand and waves gently at the camera two or three times, then lowers her hand while still smiling. Natural, subtle, realistic motion. Static camera. Keep her face, white hair, round glasses, green cardigan, armchair and sunlit living room exactly as in the original photo.

Negative prompt: distorted face, changing identity, extra fingers, warped hands, morphing glasses, fast or jerky movement, camera movement, talking, background changes

Notes for a good result
  • Keep the movement small. With real family photos, a subtle animation looks more like her; big movements tend to change the face.
  • Hands are the most likely failure point. If the wave looks wrong, regenerate, or try a version where she only raises her hand slightly from the armrest.
  • If you want, I can also make a version with a slow push-in on her face, or a looping version for sharing.

2. Structured prompt, then script

GPT-6.1 Sol

Step 1 · Structured prompt

Task

Write a production-ready script for a short image-to-video animation using the uploaded photograph. Do not generate the video itself.

Goal and Core Idea

Bring the user’s photograph of their grandmother gently to life: she transitions from a neutral expression to a warm smile and gives a small, natural wave toward the camera. The moment should feel like a friendly personal greeting, without inventing dialogue or a backstory.

User Requirements — Exact Quote
“animate this photo of my grandma so she smiles and waves, like 5 seconds”

All stated requirements must be fulfilled:

  • Animate the uploaded photograph of the user’s grandmother.
  • Show her smiling and waving.
  • Keep the video approximately five seconds long.
Audience and Platform
  • Audience: Not specified. Assume the user and their family or close friends.
  • Platform: Not specified. Treat this as a platform-neutral video suitable for personal sharing; do not add platform-specific elements.
Format, Duration, and Aspect Ratio
  • Format: A single, continuous image-to-video shot.
  • Duration: Approximately 5 seconds.
  • Aspect ratio: Preserve the uploaded photograph’s original aspect ratio and framing. Its numerical aspect ratio is not provided; do not assume one or crop unnecessarily.
Tone and Style

Warm, gentle, affectionate, and naturalistic. Use subtle, photorealistic motion rather than exaggerated cartoon animation.

Upload and Required Use

One uploaded photograph, described as:

“an elderly woman with short white hair, round glasses and a green cardigan, sitting in an armchair in a sunlit living room, looking at the camera with a neutral expression.”

Use this photograph as the sole visual reference and starting frame.

  • Preserve her recognizable facial features, age, short white hair, round glasses, and green cardigan.
  • Retain the armchair, sunlit living room, lighting, and original composition.
  • Animate her expression into a gentle smile and add a small wave with one hand.
  • Keep her seated and looking toward the camera.
  • Keep hand movement anatomically plausible and consistent with the available framing. If her hands are initially outside the frame, introduce the waving hand naturally without changing the shot.
  • Avoid facial distortion, identity drift, extra fingers, clothing changes, or unnecessary background movement.
Assumptions for Unstated Details

These are production defaults, not additional user requirements:

  • The camera remains stationary, with no cuts, zooms, or transitions.
  • The smile develops smoothly, followed by or overlapping with a brief wave.
  • The final moment holds the warm smile.
  • The video is silent, with no dialogue, music, captions, titles, or added graphics.
  • No other people, objects, or narrative events are introduced.
Missing Information Only the User Can Supply

The user has not specified:

  • The intended audience or sharing platform.
  • Any required output aspect ratio that differs from the photograph.
  • Whether sound, music, or text is desired.

These are not blockers; proceed using the assumptions above unless the user supplies different preferences.

Required Script Output

Provide a concise, timecoded script covering approximately 0–5 seconds. For each beat, describe her expression, gaze, and hand movement. Include a brief production note identifying the uploaded photo as the visual reference and the identity-preservation constraints. Do not invent spoken words or personal history.

Step 2 · Script

Animation Script — Approximately 5 Seconds

Shot: One continuous, silent shot. Camera locked; preserve the photograph’s original aspect ratio and framing.

TimeExpression and gazeHand movement
0:00–0:01Begin on the uploaded photograph’s neutral expression. She remains seated, looking toward the camera, as the corners of her mouth gently begin to lift.Hands remain in their original positions.
0:01–0:02Her expression develops smoothly into a warm, natural smile, with a subtle softening around her eyes. Gaze stays toward the camera.One hand rises slowly into a comfortable waving position, elbow bent. If initially out of frame, it enters naturally from below, without altering the shot.
0:02–0:04She holds the gentle smile and continues looking toward the camera.Give a small, relaxed wave: two gentle side-to-side wrist motions, palm toward the camera and fingers naturally spaced. Keep the movement within the existing frame.
0:04–0:05Hold the warm smile and camera-facing gaze through the final frame.The wave settles; her hand lowers slightly toward its resting position, with no abrupt movement.

Production note: Use the uploaded photograph as the sole visual reference and exact starting frame. Preserve her recognizable facial features, age, short white hair, round glasses, green cardigan, seated posture, armchair, sunlit living room, and original lighting. Keep motion subtle and photorealistic, with anatomically plausible arm, wrist, and finger movement. Avoid facial distortion, identity drift, extra fingers, clothing changes, or unnecessary background motion. No cuts, zooms, dialogue, music, text, graphics, or added people or objects.

Claude Opus 5.5

Step 1 · Structured prompt

Video Script Brief: Animated Photo of Grandma

Goal and core idea

Bring a single still photo of the user's grandmother to life. The video must show her, as she appears in the photo, breaking into a warm smile and waving at the camera. The idea to get across: a cherished, familiar image of a loved one becoming briefly "alive" — a small, affectionate moment of connection.

Audience and platform
  • Audience: The user and their family (personal, sentimental use). Not a commercial or public-facing piece.
  • Platform: Not specified. Assume it will be viewed on a phone and shared privately (messaging apps, family group chats), possibly posted to social media.
Format, duration and aspect ratio
  • Format: Single continuous shot, image-to-video animation of the uploaded photo. No cuts, no added scenes, no camera moves that reveal areas outside the original frame.
  • Duration: ~5 seconds (user said "like 5 seconds"; 4–6 seconds is acceptable).
  • Aspect ratio: Match the aspect ratio of the uploaded photo so nothing is cropped or invented at the edges. If a fixed ratio is required, use the closest standard ratio to the original (e.g. 4:3 or 3:4) and avoid cropping her face, hands or glasses.
Tone and style
  • Warm, gentle, tender, natural.
  • Photorealistic and faithful to the original photo: same lighting (sunlit living room), same colours, same grain/quality.
  • Movement must be subtle and believable for an elderly woman: slow, soft, unhurried. No exaggerated, cartoonish or uncanny motion.
  • Optional: very slight, natural secondary motion (a blink, a small head tilt, a breath, light shifting slightly) to feel alive. No dramatic camera movement; at most an almost-imperceptible slow push-in.
User requirements (quoted exactly)
  • "animate this photo of my grandma"
  • "so she smiles and waves"
  • "like 5 seconds"
Uploads and how they must be used
  • Upload: One photo — an elderly woman with short white hair, round glasses and a green cardigan, sitting in an armchair in a sunlit living room, looking at the camera with a neutral expression.
  • Usage:
  • The photo is the first frame and the only visual source. The video starts on the photo exactly as it is.
  • Her identity must be preserved precisely: face shape, features, short white hair, round glasses, green cardigan, armchair, living room and lighting must not change.
  • Action sequence within the shot: neutral expression (as in photo) → a natural, warm smile forms → she lifts one hand and gives a gentle wave toward the camera → hand lowers or settles, smile remains.
  • She stays seated in the armchair throughout. Eye contact with the camera is maintained.
  • Do not add people, objects, text, or change the background.
Assumptions (not stated by the user)
  • She waves with one hand (assume her right hand, or whichever hand is most visible/free in the photo).
  • No audio is required; if audio is added, use only quiet room ambience. No music, no voice, no speech, no lip movement as if talking.
  • No text, titles, captions or watermarks.
  • Ending: she holds the smile in the final moment (a calm end frame suitable for looping or sharing). The video does not need to return to the neutral expression.
  • Smile is closed-mouth or a gentle open smile; do not invent teeth details prominently if they are not visible in the photo.
  • The wave starts around 1.5–2 s in, after the smile has begun, and finishes by ~4.5 s.
Missing information only the user can supply
  • Where the video will be shared (affects aspect ratio and whether music is wanted).
  • Which hand she should wave with, if it matters to them.
  • Whether they want sound or music (e.g. a song that meant something to her).
  • Whether they want any text, such as her name or a short message.
  • Whether the video should loop back to her neutral expression or end on the smile.
  • Whether she is still living or this is a memorial piece, which would affect tone (e.g. softer, more nostalgic treatment).

Step 2 · Script

Script: "Grandma Waves"

Format: Single continuous shot, image-to-video from the uploaded photo Duration: ~5 seconds Aspect ratio: Match the source photo (fallback: closest of 4:3 / 3:4, no cropping of face, hands or glasses) Audio: None (optional: very quiet room ambience only — no music, no voice) On-screen text: None

Shot 1 — Continuous (0:00–0:05)

Setting (unchanged from photo): A sunlit living room. An elderly woman with short white hair, round glasses and a green cardigan sits in an armchair, facing the camera. Lighting, colours, grain and framing stay identical to the original photo throughout.

Camera: Locked off. At most, an almost-imperceptible slow push-in. No pans, no reveals beyond the original frame.

TimeActionNotes
0:00–0:01Opens on the photo exactly as uploaded. She holds her neutral expression, looking into the lens. A soft breath lifts her shoulders slightly.First frame must be pixel-faithful to the photo.
0:01–0:02A slow, natural blink. The corners of her mouth begin to lift; her cheeks rise and the skin around her eyes softens into gentle crinkles behind the glasses.Smile builds gradually, not all at once. Closed-mouth or a slight open smile — don't invent prominent teeth.
0:02–0:03The smile settles into a full, warm expression. She lifts her right hand (or whichever hand is most visible and free) from the armrest/lap to about shoulder height, palm toward camera.Movement is slow and unhurried, appropriate for her age. Cardigan sleeve moves naturally with the arm.
0:03–0:04.5She gives a gentle wave — two or three soft side-to-side motions of the hand, fingers relaxed. A tiny tilt of the head toward the camera as she waves. Eye contact never breaks.No exaggerated or cartoonish motion. Fingers must stay anatomically correct.
0:04.5–0:05Her hand lowers and comes to rest back on the armrest/lap. She holds the warm smile, eyes on the camera. Sunlight shifts very faintly across the room.Calm, still end frame on the smile — suitable for sharing or looping.
Generation prompt (single shot)
Animate this photo. The elderly woman with short white hair, round glasses and a green cardigan stays seated in her armchair in the sunlit living room, looking at the camera. She takes a soft breath, blinks slowly, and a warm, gentle smile spreads across her face. She slowly raises her right hand and gives a soft, friendly wave toward the camera, then lowers her hand to rest and keeps smiling. Subtle, natural, unhurried movement. Photorealistic, faithful to the original photo's lighting, colours and grain. Static camera, very slight slow push-in at most.

Negative / avoid: changing her face or identity, altering hair, glasses or clothing, new people or objects, background changes, text or watermarks, talking or lip-sync movement, fast or jerky motion, exaggerated expressions, distorted hands or extra fingers, camera moves that reveal outside the original frame.

Open questions for you
  • Where will you share it? This decides the final aspect ratio and whether music makes sense.
  • Which hand should she wave with, if it matters?
  • Sound or music? A song that meant something to her could be lovely — or keep it silent.
  • Any text? For example her name or a short message at the end.
  • Ending: hold on the smile (current plan), or ease back to her neutral expression so it loops seamlessly?
  • Tone: is this a memorial piece? If so, I'd soften the treatment slightly — a gentler, more nostalgic pace and possibly a slow fade at the end.

3. Story first, then operational chunks

GPT-6.1 Sol

Step 1 · Story

In a sunlit living room, your grandma sits comfortably in her armchair, wearing a green cardigan and round glasses, her short white hair softly lit by the daylight. She begins with the same neutral expression as in the photo, looking toward you. Over about five seconds, her face brightens into a gentle smile. She lifts one hand and gives a small, friendly wave, then settles back, still smiling. The moment feels warm and familiar, like a quiet hello.

Step 2 · Operational chunks

Operational chunks

These chunks form one continuous animation of the uploaded photo, not separate shots.

Chunk 1: Neutral expression to beginning smile
  • Duration: 1.5 seconds
  • Visual: Begin with the uploaded photo’s original composition and neutral expression. Grandma remains seated, looking at the camera. Her expression gradually softens and a gentle smile begins.
  • On-screen text: None.
  • Audio: No narration, dialogue, music or added sound effects.
  • Entities: Grandma: preserve her exact likeness, short white hair, round glasses and green cardigan. Preserve the armchair and all visible objects from the photo.
  • Environment: The original sunlit living room. Preserve the photo’s daylight, soft lighting, colors and background.
  • Depends on: None; the uploaded photo is the starting frame and identity reference.
  • Source: “In a sunlit living room, your grandma sits comfortably in her armchair, wearing a green cardigan and round glasses, her short white hair softly lit by the daylight. She begins with the same neutral expression as in the photo, looking toward you. Over about five seconds, her face brightens into a gentle smile.”
Chunk 2: Gentle smile and friendly wave
  • Duration: 2.5 seconds
  • Visual: Continue the smile naturally. Grandma lifts one hand and gives a small, friendly wave toward the camera. Use whichever hand can move most naturally within the source photo’s framing; keep the same hand throughout the gesture. Keep her seated posture and gaze stable.
  • On-screen text: None.
  • Audio: No narration, dialogue, music or added sound effects.
  • Entities: The same grandma, with unchanged facial identity, short white hair, round glasses and green cardigan. The moving hand must remain anatomically consistent with her body. Preserve the original armchair and background objects.
  • Environment: The same sunlit living room, with unchanged daylight, lighting and palette.
  • Depends on: Chunk 1’s final frame; match expression, posture, framing and lighting without a visible transition.
  • Source: “Over about five seconds, her face brightens into a gentle smile. She lifts one hand and gives a small, friendly wave, then settles back, still smiling.”
Chunk 3: Settle back, still smiling
  • Duration: 1 second
  • Visual: Grandma finishes the wave, lowers that same hand naturally and settles back into her seated pose. End with her looking at the camera and holding a gentle smile.
  • On-screen text: None.
  • Audio: No narration, dialogue, music or added sound effects.
  • Entities: The same grandma, preserving her exact likeness, short white hair, round glasses and green cardigan. Preserve the armchair and original room objects.
  • Environment: The same sunlit living room, with stable lighting, colors and background.
  • Depends on: Chunk 2’s final frame; continue the hand movement smoothly and preserve her smile.
  • Source: “She lifts one hand and gives a small, friendly wave, then settles back, still smiling. The moment feels warm and familiar, like a quiet hello.”
Global requirements
  • Total duration: Approximately 5 seconds; planned timing is 5 seconds.
  • Aspect ratio: Match the uploaded photo’s native aspect ratio and framing.
  • Identity: Animate the actual grandma in the uploaded photo. Do not replace or redesign her face, hair, glasses, clothing, body or surroundings.
  • Required action: Begin with the photo’s neutral expression, develop a gentle smile, give a small wave with one hand, then settle back still smiling.
  • Music and audio: None. “A quiet hello” describes the feeling, not spoken dialogue.
  • Style: Natural, photorealistic photo animation with subtle, believable movement and a warm, familiar mood.
  • Continuity: Fixed camera; seamless motion across chunks. No cuts, zooms, background changes, extra people, captions or overlays.
  • Animation quality: Preserve stable facial features and glasses. Avoid flicker, warped hands, extra fingers and unnatural movement.

Claude Opus 5.5

Step 1 · Story

Grandma's Wave — a 5-second moment

Afternoon sun pours through the living-room window and settles in a warm stripe across the armchair. Dust drifts slowly in the light. Grandma sits as she always does, deep in the cushions, her short white hair soft and bright where the sun catches it. Her round glasses hold two small reflections of the window. Her green cardigan is buttoned to the middle, the wool slightly fuzzy at the cuffs.

For a heartbeat she is still, looking straight ahead with that calm, neutral expression of someone waiting for the photo to be over. Then something changes. The corners of her eyes crinkle first, behind the glasses, and the smile follows — slow and genuine, lifting her cheeks and softening her whole face. It is the smile of someone who has just recognized a person she loves.

Her right hand rises from the arm of the chair. It lifts to about shoulder height, palm open, and she waves — two or three gentle side-to-side movements, unhurried, the way grandmothers wave from a doorway. Her head tilts very slightly as she does it. The cardigan sleeve slides a little down her wrist.

She holds the smile as the wave settles, her hand pausing in the air for a final second. The room stays quiet and golden around her. Nothing else moves except the drifting dust and the warmth in her face.

The whole moment lasts about five seconds — a small, tender greeting from her chair, as if she has just seen you walk in.

Step 2 · Operational chunks

Grandma's Wave: Operational Chunks

The story is one continuous 5-second moment from a single photo. I split it into three chunks so each action beat can be checked on its own. In production they should be generated as one continuous image-to-video clip from the uploaded photo, so there are no cuts. Each chunk's end frame is the next chunk's start frame.

Chunk 1: Still moment

  • Duration: ~1.0 s (0.0–1.0 s)
  • Visual: The uploaded photo comes to life. Grandma sits in the armchair, looking at the camera with her neutral expression from the photo. Barely-there motion: dust drifting in the sunbeam, a soft breath, a natural blink at most. Framing and camera position match the photo exactly. The camera is static.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Quiet room tone. No narration, no dialogue. No music (none requested).
  • Entities:
  • Grandma (identity locked to the uploaded photo): elderly woman, short white hair, round glasses, green cardigan buttoned to the middle with slightly fuzzy cuffs. Same face, same features, same clothes, same body position in the chair.
  • Armchair: same chair, same position as in the photo.
  • Environment: Sunlit living room, afternoon. Warm sunlight from the window falls in a stripe across the armchair. Visible drifting dust. Warm, golden palette, as in the photo.
  • Depends on: None. The first frame is the uploaded photo.
  • Source: "Afternoon sun pours through the living-room window and settles in a warm stripe across the armchair. Dust drifts slowly in the light. Grandma sits as she always does, deep in the cushions… Her green cardigan is buttoned to the middle, the wool slightly fuzzy at the cuffs." / "For a heartbeat she is still, looking straight ahead with that calm, neutral expression…"

Chunk 2: The smile

  • Duration: ~1.5 s (1.0–2.5 s)
  • Visual: Her eyes crinkle behind the glasses first. Then a slow, genuine smile spreads, her cheeks lift, and her whole face softens. Her hands stay on the chair arms. Her head stays in place. The camera stays static.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Room tone continues. Optional very soft ambient (no added music).
  • Entities: Grandma, as in Chunk 1. The smile must look like her smile. Do not change face shape, teeth, glasses or hair. Reflections in the glasses stay consistent.
  • Environment: Same as Chunk 1, with no change in light.
  • Depends on: Chunk 1 end frame (continuous, no cut).
  • Source: "Then something changes. The corners of her eyes crinkle first, behind the glasses, and the smile follows — slow and genuine, lifting her cheeks and softening her whole face."

Chunk 3: The wave and hold

  • Duration: ~2.5 s (2.5–5.0 s)
  • Visual: Still smiling, she lifts her right hand from the arm of the chair to about shoulder height, palm open. She waves 2–3 gentle side-to-side motions, unhurried. Her head tilts very slightly. The cardigan sleeve slides a little down her wrist. The wave settles, her hand pauses in the air, and she holds the smile through the last ~1 second. The camera stays static.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Room tone. Optional faint fabric rustle on the wave.
  • Entities: Grandma, as in Chunks 1–2. Her right hand must have correct anatomy (five fingers, natural proportions). The cardigan sleeve stays green with the same texture.
  • Environment: Same as Chunk 1. Only the dust and Grandma move.
  • Depends on: Chunk 2 end frame (continuous, no cut).
  • Source: "Her right hand rises from the arm of the chair. It lifts to about shoulder height, palm open, and she waves — two or three gentle side-to-side movements… Her head tilts very slightly as she does it. The cardigan sleeve slides a little down her wrist." / "She holds the smile as the wave settles, her hand pausing in the air for a final second… Nothing else moves except the drifting dust and the warmth in her face."
Global requirements
  • Total duration: about 5 seconds ("like 5 seconds"). Target 5.0 s.
  • Source and identity: Animate this photo. The uploaded photo is the first frame and the identity reference. Grandma must look exactly like herself throughout: face, short white hair, round glasses, green cardigan, pose and armchair. No beautification, de-aging or restyling.
  • Required actions: She smiles and she waves. Both must be clearly visible.
  • Aspect ratio: Keep the uploaded photo's original aspect ratio and framing. Do not crop or extend.
  • Camera: Static, locked to the photo's viewpoint. No cuts. One continuous shot.
  • Style: Photorealistic. Match the photo's color, grain and lighting. Subtle, natural motion only. The background stays still except for drifting dust.
  • Audio: No narration, dialogue or music was requested. Use quiet room tone only, or deliver silent.
  • Lighting and palette: Warm, golden afternoon sunlight from the window, as in the photo. Constant across the clip.

4. Director's shooting script

GPT-6.1 Sol

Shooting Script — “Grandma’s Hello”

Duration: 5 seconds Source: Uploaded photograph Style: Gentle, naturalistic photo animation; preserve Grandma’s likeness, clothing, and the sunlit room.

Scene 1 — Living Room, Day
Shot 1 — Continuous Portrait

Timing: 0:00–0:05 Shot type: Medium portrait, matching the photograph’s original framing. Camera movement: Locked-off; no zoom, pan, or cut.

TimeAction
0.0–0.8 secBegin with the original pose: Grandma sits in the armchair, looking into the camera with a neutral expression. Add only subtle breathing.
0.8–1.8 secHer expression gradually becomes a warm smile. Her cheeks lift gently as she raises one hand beside her shoulder, palm toward the camera.
1.8–4.2 secMaintaining eye contact and her smile, she gives two small, relaxed side-to-side waves. Keep the wrist movement soft and the fingers naturally curved.
4.2–5.0 secThe wave finishes. Her hand begins to settle lower while she holds the warm smile through the final frame.

On-screen text: None. Dialogue / narration: None. Sound: Faint, steady indoor room tone; no added gesture sounds. Music: None.

Direction / animation notes: Keep her white hair, round glasses, green cardigan, armchair, and background stable. Preserve the existing sunlight. Use restrained facial movement and anatomically natural hand motion—no exaggerated grin, body sway, or background warping.

Claude Opus 5.5

"Hello, Love" — Shooting Script

Runtime: 5 seconds Source: Your uploaded photo (elderly woman, short white hair, round glasses, green cardigan, armchair, sunlit living room) Aspect ratio: Match the original photo Guiding rule: She must stay recognizably herself. Same face, same glasses, same cardigan, same chair, same room, same light. Only the expression and the hand move.

SCENE 1 — INT. LIVING ROOM — DAY (one continuous shot)
Shot 1 — Medium shot, static with a very slow push-in
Timing0.0 s – 5.0 s
Shot typeMedium shot, framed exactly as the uploaded photo (seated, waist up, armchair visible)
Camera movementLocked-off for the first second, then a barely perceptible push-in (about 3–5% zoom over the remaining 4 seconds). No pan, no tilt. The frame should feel like the photo coming to life, not a camera moving through the room.
On-screen textNone
Dialogue / narrationNone

Action, beat by beat:

  • 0.0 – 1.0 s — Stillness into life. The image holds on her neutral expression, exactly as in the photo. Subtle signs of life begin: a slow breath lifts her shoulders slightly, a natural blink at around 0.8 s. Dust motes drift gently in the sunlight.
  • 1.0 – 2.2 s — The smile. Her eyes warm first: a slight crinkle at the corners behind the round glasses. Then the smile spreads, cheeks lifting, lips parting just a little. It should be a soft, genuine, closed-to-slightly-open smile, the kind you give someone you're happy to see. Her head tilts a few degrees to one side.
  • 2.2 – 4.2 s — The wave. Her right hand rises from the armrest or her lap to about shoulder height, palm facing the camera. She waves two or three times with a gentle, unhurried side-to-side motion from the wrist and forearm. The movement is slow and relaxed, as fits her age. Fingers stay natural; the cardigan sleeve moves with her arm.
  • 4.2 – 5.0 s — Settle. Her hand lowers slightly and comes to rest near her chest or back toward her lap. She holds the warm smile, looking straight into the lens. Gentle final blink at around 4.6 s. End on the smile.

Lighting: Keep the original sunlight direction and warmth. Allow faint, natural shifts as she moves (light catching her glasses, highlights on her white hair), but no change in time of day or color.

Sound and music:

  • Ambience (0.0 – 5.0 s): Quiet room tone. A distant clock tick and faint birdsong outside the window.
  • Music (0.8 – 5.0 s): A soft, warm piano or music-box phrase. Three or four notes that rise as she smiles and resolve on the final hold. Low volume, intimate.
  • Foley: The faint rustle of the cardigan sleeve as her arm lifts (around 2.3 s).
  • Out: Music note rings out and fades over the last half second.
Continuity and quality checklist
  • Face shape, features, and age match the photo in every frame. No smoothing, no de-aging.
  • Round glasses stay on, same frame shape, no warping during the head tilt.
  • Short white hair keeps its shape and texture.
  • Green cardigan keeps its color, texture, and buttons.
  • Hand has five fingers and natural proportions throughout the wave.
  • Armchair, room, and background objects do not drift, morph, or move.
  • Motion is slow and natural. No sudden or exaggerated movements.

Optional variants if you'd like options:

  • Silent version: Ambience only, no music. Feels more like a real home movie.
  • Seamless loop: End frame returns to the neutral expression from frame one, so it can loop as a living photo.

5. Story, then change-based chunks

GPT-6.1 Sol

Step 2 · Change-based chunks (step 1 is the story from approach 3)

Baseline — first frame

Step 1 · Structured prompt

Video Script Brief: Sweet Crumbs Bakery Ad

Goal and core idea

Create a short promotional ad for a local bakery called Sweet Crumbs. The ad must make viewers crave fresh-baked goods and want to visit, and it must leave them knowing exactly when the bakery is open and where it is. Core idea: fresh, warm, homemade baking every morning, just down the street — come in.

Audience and platform
  • Audience: Local residents, commuters and passers-by near 12 Elm St; people looking for breakfast, coffee-and-pastry, or baked treats. Broad adult audience.
  • Platform: Not specified. Assume social media (Instagram/Facebook Reels, TikTok) and possibly local digital displays. Must work with the sound off.
Format, duration and aspect ratio
  • Format: Short commercial: a sequence of short shots of the bakery and its products, ending on a static end card.
  • Duration: 20 seconds total (stated by user). Suggested split: ~16 s of footage, ~4 s end card (end card must stay on screen long enough to read comfortably).
  • Aspect ratio: 9:16 vertical (assumed, for social). Keep key action and all text inside a central safe area so a 1:1 or 4:5 crop also works.
Tone and style
  • Warm, inviting, cosy, appetising, neighbourhood-friendly.
  • Visual style: soft morning light, golden tones, close-ups of textures (flaky crusts, steam, dusting of sugar, crumb), hands at work (kneading, sliding trays from the oven), a welcoming storefront/counter.
  • Pacing: gentle but steady; 5–7 shots before the end card.
  • Audio: light, upbeat acoustic music; optional subtle ambient sounds (oven door, bell over the door). No voiceover required (see assumptions).
User requirements (quoted exactly)
  • "ad for my bakery"
  • "20 sec"
  • "the end card must say exactly 'Sweet Crumbs — Open 7am–3pm, 12 Elm St'"
  • "dont spell anything wrong"
Critical text rule

The end card text must be reproduced character for character:

Sweet Crumbs — Open 7am–3pm, 12 Elm St
  • Keep the em dash (—) after "Sweet Crumbs" and the en dash (–) in "7am–3pm".
  • Keep lowercase "am"/"pm" with no spaces or periods, the comma after "3pm", and "St" without a period.
  • Do not add, remove, reword, abbreviate or re-punctuate anything.
  • The end card text should be rendered as a crisp text overlay/graphic, not generated inside AI imagery, so it cannot be misspelled.
  • Any other on-screen text in the ad must also be spelled correctly. Prefer minimal or no other on-screen text to reduce risk; the bakery name, if shown elsewhere, must be exactly "Sweet Crumbs".
  • No legible signage, labels or text should appear inside generated footage (e.g. storefront signs, packaging), since it may be misspelled.
Uploads and how they must be used
  • Uploads: None.
  • All visuals must be newly created. Since there is no logo, storefront photo or product photo, the bakery and its products must be generic and must not imply a specific real location or brand look.
Assumptions (not stated by the user)
  • Products shown: a general mix of common bakery items — croissants, bread loaves, muffins, cinnamon rolls, cookies, coffee alongside pastries.
  • Emphasis on mornings (fits the 7am opening): sunrise light, first batch out of the oven, early customers.
  • No voiceover and no dialogue; music only. Message carried visually plus the end card.
  • No logo exists; the end card uses clean, warm typography (e.g. a friendly serif or hand-lettered-style font) on a warm neutral or brand-free background.
  • No prices, offers or promotions.
  • Target region is English-speaking; time format as given by user.
  • People shown (baker, customers) are generic; no specific identity.
Missing information only the user can supply
  • Logo, brand colours or fonts, if they have them.
  • Real photos or footage of the shop, storefront, staff or products (to make the ad look like their actual bakery).
  • Signature or best-selling products to feature.
  • Where the ad will run (to confirm aspect ratio and whether sound will be heard).
  • Whether they want a voiceover, a tagline, or a call to action beyond the end card (e.g. website, phone, social handle, "order ahead").
  • Which days the 7am–3pm hours apply to (the end card is fixed as written, but the user should confirm it is accurate).
  • Any special offer, launch, or seasonal theme to mention.

Warm, welcoming smile from the baker, slightly out of focus behind. Sound: Door bell jingle. Coffee cup set down on saucer.

Visual: Clean, warm neutral background (soft cream or light toast colour), optionally with a subtle blurred still of the pastry case behind it. Text appears with a gentle fade-in and holds, fully static, for at least 3 seconds.

Sweet Crumbs — Open 7am–3pm, 12 Elm St

Sound: Music resolves on a soft final chord and fades out by 0:20.

Step 1 · Story

In this twenty-second ad, morning sunlight warms Sweet Crumbs, a cozy bakery with wooden shelves and a glass counter full of golden pastries. A baker in a flour-dusted apron lifts a tray of croissants from the oven. Their crisp, buttery layers glisten beside soft cinnamon rolls and thick chocolate-chip cookies.

A customer steps inside as the doorbell gently chimes. The baker smiles and hands over a small pastry box. The customer breaks open a warm croissant, revealing its tender center, and takes a delighted bite. The mood is comforting, cheerful, and inviting—a little sweetness to start the day.

The ad ends with an end card displaying exactly:

Sweet Crumbs — Open 7am–3pm, 12 Elm St

Chunk plan — 20 seconds total
Chunk 1: Morning at Sweet Crumbs

  • Environment: Bakery interior, morning. Honey-colored wood, cream walls, warm natural sunlight.
  • Depends on: None.
  • Source: “In this twenty-second ad, morning sunlight warms Sweet Crumbs, a cozy bakery with wooden shelves and a glass counter full of golden pastries.”

  • Environment: Baking area within the same bakery. Warm morning light mixed with a soft amber oven glow.
  • Depends on: Chunk 1 for bakery materials, palette, and lighting.
  • Source: “A baker in a flour-dusted apron lifts a tray of croissants from the oven.”

  • Environment: Same bakery counter, morning. Warm side lighting emphasizes buttery layers and golden surfaces.
  • Depends on: Chunk 2 for the tray and croissants; Chunk 1 for the counter and surrounding bakery.
  • Source: “Their crisp, buttery layers glisten beside soft cinnamon rolls and thick chocolate-chip cookies.”

  • Depends on: Chunk 2 for baker identity; Chunk 4 for customer identity; Chunk 1 for the counter.
  • Source: “The baker smiles and hands over a small pastry box.”

  • Duration: 3 seconds · 0:17–0:20

Claude Opus 5.5

Step 1 · Story

Sweet Crumbs — a 20-second ad

It is still dark outside, just before seven. Inside Sweet Crumbs, a single row of warm pendant lights glows over a flour-dusted wooden counter. The ovens hum. A baker in a cream apron presses her palms into a soft round of dough, folding it over itself with practiced, easy movements. Flour lifts into the air and hangs in the light like fine snow.

An oven door swings open and a wave of heat rolls out. Inside, a tray of croissants has turned deep golden, their layers puffed and crackling. Beside them, a loaf of sourdough splits along its scored top, the crust blistered and dark at the edges. The baker slides the tray out and sets it on the cooling rack. Steam rises from everything.

Morning arrives. Pale blue light fills the front windows. The display case is now full: glossy fruit tarts, cinnamon rolls swirled with white icing, rows of cookies with crisp edges, a tall chocolate cake with a single slice already missing. The bell above the door rings. A woman steps in from the street, pulls off her scarf, and breathes in. Her shoulders drop. She smiles before she has even reached the counter.

At a small table by the window, a father and his young daughter share a cinnamon roll. The girl tears off the soft center, and a thin ribbon of icing stretches between her fingers. She laughs. Her father brushes a crumb from her cheek. Outside, the street is waking up, but in here everything feels slow and warm.

The light settles. The bakery's warm tones soften behind a clean end card. The text appears, crisp and centered, spelled exactly:

Sweet Crumbs — Open 7am–3pm, 12 Elm St

The card holds for the final seconds, and the ad ends at twenty seconds.

Production notes
  • Logo accuracy: AI video models often distort small logos and text. For best results, generate each shot from your product photo as a reference, check every frame, and regenerate any shot where the logo or "VOLT" text warps. For the hero shot (Shot 6), you can composite the original product photo directly onto the generated background to guarantee a perfect match.
  • End text: Add "VOLT. CHARGE UP." as a text overlay in editing, not inside the generated video. The tagline is a placeholder — send me your real tagline if you have one.
  • Hands: In Shots 3 and 4, keep fingers off the logo.

3. Story first, then operational chunks

GPT-6.1 Sol

Step 1 · Story

A matte black 355 ml can stands alone on a plain white surface against a plain white background. It looks exactly like the supplied product photo: the neon green lightning-bolt logo and the word VOLT in white capital letters are fully visible. The space is immaculate and silent, with the can’s dark finish giving it a bold, unmistakable presence.

A thin line of green light appears on the surface beside the can. It advances like a spark finding its path, tracing an angular route around the base. The can remains unchanged, its neon green lightning-bolt logo and white VOLT lettering clearly visible. The stillness begins to feel charged.

The white surroundings darken to charcoal, revealing the surface as a smooth platform in a vast, empty room. Green light spreads outward through narrow grooves in the floor. At the center stands the same matte black 355 ml can, exactly matching the reference, with its lightning-bolt logo and VOLT lettering unobstructed. A low vibration builds through the room.

The illuminated grooves flare into branching patterns, racing toward the walls. Brief green sparks leap between distant metal structures, filling the space with restless movement. The can remains steady amid the activity, its original matte black finish, neon green lightning-bolt logo, and white VOLT lettering intact and fully visible.

A bright green pulse sweeps through the room. The floor’s scattered lines light up together, forming one enormous lightning-bolt pattern beneath the platform. Everything that was dormant now feels alive. The unchanged can stands at the heart of it, its own neon green lightning-bolt logo and white VOLT lettering plainly visible.

The pulse fades, and the room returns to pure white. The same matte black 355 ml can remains alone on the plain white surface, looking exactly like the supplied photo, with the neon green lightning-bolt logo and white VOLT lettering fully visible. The last green glimmer disappears beside its base, leaving a clean, confident stillness.

Step 2 · Operational chunks

Production plan: 6 shots / 6 chunks

Each chunk is one continuous shot. Estimated total duration: 30 seconds. The supplied product photo is the identity reference; environmental effects must never alter or obscure the can.

Chunk 1: Clean Hero
  • Duration: 5 seconds
  • Visual: The can stands alone, centered on a plain white surface against a plain white background. Match its appearance and front-facing orientation to the supplied photo. Hold a static product composition with the entire can visible.
  • On-screen text: “VOLT” — existing white lettering on the can only. No added text.
  • Audio: No narration, dialogue, music, or sound effects. Complete silence.
  • Entities: One matte black 355 ml can, exactly matching the reference photo. Preserve its proportions, finish, neon green lightning-bolt logo, white VOLT lettering, and label layout. Logo and lettering remain fully visible.
  • Environment: Immaculate white studio setting; time of day unspecified. Soft, neutral product lighting; white, matte black, and neon green palette.
  • Depends on: None.
  • Source: “A matte black 355 ml can stands alone on a plain white surface against a plain white background. It looks exactly like the supplied product photo: the neon green lightning-bolt logo and the word VOLT in white capital letters are fully visible. The space is immaculate and silent, with the can’s dark finish giving it a bold, unmistakable presence.”
Chunk 2: First Spark
  • Duration: 5 seconds
  • Visual: Begin with Chunk 1’s final composition. A thin green light appears beside the can, then advances along an angular route around its base. Keep the light on the surface; it must not cross the front of the can or cover its branding. The can remains motionless.
  • On-screen text: “VOLT” — existing white lettering on the can only. No added text.
  • Audio: No narration, dialogue, or music. Introduce a faint electrical tracing sound as the green line advances.
  • Entities: The same reference-matched matte black 355 ml can, with unchanged neon green lightning-bolt logo and white VOLT lettering, fully visible. One thin neon green light trail.
  • Environment: Same plain white surface and background, with unchanged neutral product lighting. A restrained green glow appears near the base.
  • Depends on: Chunk 1’s final frame: match can placement, scale, orientation, lighting, and surface.
  • Source: “A thin line of green light appears on the surface beside the can. It advances like a spark finding its path, tracing an angular route around the base. The can remains unchanged, its neon green lightning-bolt logo and white VOLT lettering clearly visible. The stillness begins to feel charged.”
Chunk 3: Room Awakens
  • Duration: 5 seconds
  • Visual: Without an internal cut, the white surroundings darken to charcoal, revealing a smooth platform in a vast room. Green light spreads outward through narrow floor grooves. Use a restrained camera pullback to reveal the setting while keeping the can large enough for its logo and lettering to remain clearly readable.
  • On-screen text: “VOLT” — existing white lettering on the can only. No added text.

Claude Opus 5.5

Step 1 · Story

VOLT — a product ad in six shots

The can is the same in every moment of this film: a matte black 355 ml can, its surface soft and non-reflective, with a neon green lightning-bolt logo and the word VOLT in white capital letters. It looks exactly as it does in the product photo, and the logo is clearly visible in every one of the six shots.

One. Darkness. Then a single thin line of neon green appears, flickering like a live wire. It sharpens into the lightning bolt, and the matte black can emerges around it from the shadows, standing upright. The white letters V-O-L-T catch the light last. A low electric hum builds underneath.

Two. The can stands on a wet concrete rooftop at night. The city glows far below. Rain falls in fine streaks around it, and drops bead on the matte surface without making it shine. The green bolt and the white VOLT face forward, steady and bright against the dark skyline.

Three. A hand reaches in and grips the can, fingers wrapped low so the logo and the name stay fully visible. The thumb finds the tab. A sharp crack and hiss break the silence, and a faint mist escapes from the opening. The sound lands like a starting gun.

Four. Energy breaks loose. A skateboarder launches off a concrete ledge, a runner bursts off the line, a dancer spins under strobing green light. In the middle of the motion, the can sits on the ledge, in the runner's hand, on the edge of the stage — always upright, always facing out, the neon bolt pulsing with the rhythm.

Five. The can is suspended mid-air against pure black. Fine droplets drift around it in slow motion. Arcs of neon green electricity crawl across the background, echoing the shape of the logo. The can turns slightly, then settles facing forward, the lightning bolt and VOLT perfectly sharp.

Six. Silence again. The can stands alone on a plain white surface, just as in the product photo: matte black, neon green bolt, white VOLT lettering. The hum fades out. The can holds still, clean and confident, as the ad ends.

Step 2 · Operational chunks

VOLT: Operational Chunks (6 shots)

One chunk = one shot. The request asks for exactly 6 shots, so there are exactly 6 chunks.

Can reference (applies to every chunk): a matte black 355 ml can, non-reflective surface, neon green lightning-bolt logo, the word VOLT in white capital letters. It must match the uploaded product photo exactly. The logo must be clearly visible and correct in every shot. Use the product photo as the reference image for every generation. Composite or swap in the real can image where the generated can drifts.

Chunk 1: Ignition

  • Duration: ~3 s
  • Visual: Pure darkness. A thin neon green line flickers like a live wire, then sharpens into the lightning-bolt logo. The matte black can emerges from the shadows around it, upright, logo facing the camera. The white VOLT lettering lights up last.
  • On-screen text: None. VOLT appears only as the printed logo on the can.
  • Audio: Silence, then a low electric hum builds. Faint electrical crackle on the flicker.
  • Entities: VOLT can (see can reference). Front-facing, upright, logo fully visible.
  • Environment: Black void. The only light is a neon green glow plus a subtle rim light on the can. Palette: black, neon green, white.
  • Depends on: None. Final frame must show the can and logo clearly.
  • Source: "Darkness. Then a single thin line of neon green appears, flickering like a live wire. It sharpens into the lightning bolt, and the matte black can emerges around it from the shadows, standing upright. The white letters V-O-L-T catch the light last. A low electric hum builds underneath."

Chunk 2: Rooftop rain

  • Duration: ~3 s
  • Visual: The can stands on a wet concrete rooftop at night. The city glows far below, out of focus. Fine rain falls in streaks. Drops bead on the can but the surface stays matte, not glossy. The bolt and VOLT face forward.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Hum continues under rain patter. Distant city ambience.
  • Entities: VOLT can (see can reference). Must stay matte despite the rain. Logo facing the camera.
  • Environment: Rooftop, night, rain. Cool city bokeh, wet concrete. Palette: deep blue-black, neon green accent, white.
  • Depends on: Chunk 1 (same can, same orientation logic).
  • Source: "The can stands on a wet concrete rooftop at night. The city glows far below. Rain falls in fine streaks around it, and drops bead on the matte surface without making it shine. The green bolt and the white VOLT face forward, steady and bright against the dark skyline."

Chunk 3: The crack

  • Duration: ~2.5 s
  • Visual: Close-up. A hand grips the can low, so the fingers do not cover the logo or the name. The thumb lifts the tab. The can cracks open and a faint mist escapes.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Silence, then a sharp crack and hiss, hard and loud, like a starting gun. Music beat drops right after.
  • Entities: VOLT can (see can reference). Logo and VOLT fully unobstructed. Hand: neutral, correct anatomy. Grip stays low on the can.
  • Environment: Rooftop at night, tight framing, background dark. Same palette as Chunk 2.
  • Depends on: Chunk 2 (same location and lighting).
  • Source: "A hand reaches in and grips the can, fingers wrapped low so the logo and the name stay fully visible. The thumb finds the tab. A sharp crack and hiss break the silence, and a faint mist escapes from the opening. The sound lands like a starting gun."

Chunk 4: Energy montage

  • Duration: ~4 s
  • Visual: One shot with fast, energetic movement. Option A: a single continuous move that passes the three actions. Option B: rapid in-camera whip transitions. A skateboarder launches off a concrete ledge, a runner bursts off the line, a dancer spins under strobing green light. The can is visible throughout: on the ledge, in the runner's hand, on the edge of the stage. It is always upright, always facing out, with the bolt pulsing with the rhythm.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Driving electronic beat at full energy. Skateboard pop, sneaker scuff, crowd or strobe whoosh.
  • Entities: VOLT can (see can reference), upright, logo facing out in each moment. Skateboarder, runner and dancer (unbranded clothing, no competing logos).
  • Environment: Urban concrete, track and stage. Night. Strobing neon green light. Palette: black, neon green, white.
  • Depends on: Chunk 3 (the beat drop from the can opening leads into this shot).
  • Source: "Energy breaks loose. A skateboarder launches off a concrete ledge, a runner bursts off the line, a dancer spins under strobing green light. In the middle of the motion, the can sits on the ledge, in the runner's hand, on the edge of the stage — always upright, always facing out, the neon bolt pulsing with the rhythm."
  • Note: This is the hardest shot for the "6 shots" rule. To count as one shot, build it as a continuous move or a single shot with in-camera transitions, not as three separate cuts.

Chunk 5: Suspended hero

  • Duration: ~3 s
  • Visual: The can floats mid-air against pure black. Fine droplets drift around it in slow motion. Arcs of neon green electricity crawl across the background, echoing the bolt shape. The can rotates slightly, then settles facing forward, with the bolt and VOLT perfectly sharp.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Music drops into slow-motion texture. Electrical crackle on the arcs.
  • Entities: VOLT can (see can reference). Any rotation must end with the logo fully front-facing and in sharp focus.
  • Environment: Black void, neon green electric arcs, backlit droplets.
  • Depends on: Chunk 1 (same void and green-arc visual language).
  • Source: "The can is suspended mid-air against pure black. Fine droplets drift around it in slow motion. Arcs of neon green electricity crawl across the background, echoing the shape of the logo. The can turns slightly, then settles facing forward, the lightning bolt and VOLT perfectly sharp."

Chunk 6: Packshot

  • Duration: ~3 s
  • Visual: Silence. The can stands alone on a plain white surface, matching the uploaded product photo: matte black, neon green bolt, white VOLT lettering. It holds still to the end.
  • On-screen text: None (none in the story).
  • Audio: Music cuts. The hum fades to silence.
  • Entities: VOLT can, identical to the product photo. Best practice: use the actual product photo (cleaned and extended to the frame) as this shot's source.
  • Environment: Plain white background, soft even studio light, as in the product photo.
  • Depends on: The uploaded product photo (direct match). Chunk 5 (the can settles front-facing, then the cut to white).
  • Source: "Silence again. The can stands alone on a plain white surface, just as in the product photo: matte black, neon green bolt, white VOLT lettering. The hum fades out. The can holds still, clean and confident, as the ad ends."
Global requirements
  • Shot count: exactly 6 shots. No extra cuts inside a shot.
  • Can identity (hard requirement): the can must look exactly like the uploaded photo in every shot: matte black, 355 ml proportions, neon green lightning-bolt logo, white capital VOLT. No gloss, no color drift, no altered or misspelled lettering, no added text on the can.
  • Logo in every shot: the logo must be clearly visible in all 6 shots. It must not be obscured by hands, rain, mist or motion blur. QC every shot against the product photo before delivery.
  • Total duration: not specified. Estimate ~18 s (6 shots of 2.5–4 s). Adjust to fit a 15 s or 20 s spot if needed.
  • Aspect ratio: not specified. Default to 16:9. Offer 9:16 and 1:1 cutdowns for social.
  • Music and sound: an electric hum motif opens and closes the ad. A driving electronic beat drops at the can opening (Chunk 3) and peaks in Chunk 4. Silence at the end. No narration or dialogue.
  • Style: high-contrast, premium product commercial. Black, neon green and white palette. Crisp focus on the can in every shot.

VOLT — “Turn It On”

Format: 20-second product advertisement · 3 scenes · 6 shots Visual style: Matte-black studio, sharp white highlights, neon-green accents.

Product continuity — mandatory in every shot: Match the supplied photo’s can shape, proportions, matte-black finish, neon-green lightning-bolt logo, and white VOLT lettering exactly. Keep the reference-facing side toward camera, with both the bolt and wordmark visible, readable, and unobstructed throughout. Use photo-derived artwork for final compositing; do not redraw the logo or introduce new label details. Added advertising text stays separate from the can.

Setting: Black studio. Can upright on a dark, low-reflection pedestal.

  • On-screen text: TURN IT ON. appears below the can, outside its silhouette.
  • Dialogue / narration: VO: “VOLT.”
  • Sound: One clean, low-frequency impact on the brand name.
  • Music: Rhythm drops away, leaving a sustained electronic tone.
SHOT 6 — Final Packshot

Timing: 0:17–0:20 · 3 seconds

  • Shot type: Locked-off full-product packshot, straight-on.
  • Camera movement: None.
  • Action: Hold the exact reference-matched can, fully visible, with generous negative space. Lighting and label remain steady through the final frame; no fade obscures the product.
  • On-screen text: VOLT and TURN IT ON. beside the can, using separate campaign typography.
  • Dialogue / narration: VO: “Turn it on.”
  • Sound: Short electrical snap, followed by a clean sonic-logo tail.
  • Music: Resolves on a final bass note.

Cut to end at exactly 20 seconds.
