Lighthouse short film

“A short film, 2 minutes. A lighthouse keeper finds a glowing shell on the beach at dawn. She brings it home. At night it hums and the lamp goes out. She climbs the tower in a storm and uses the shell to relight it. Ships are saved. Morning again, she puts the shell back on the beach. Melancholy, painterly, no dialogue, just score.”

1. One-shot script

GPT-6.1 Sol

BORROWED LIGHT

Short film · 2 minutes · No dialogue

Painterly images: slate-blue sea, bruised clouds, pale gold light. Soft edges, lingering compositions.

Sound: instrumental score only—sparse piano and low strings. The shell’s hum is expressed as a sustained musical tone. No voices or sound effects.

0:00–0:20 — EXT. BEACH — DAWN

The lighthouse stands above a deserted shore, its beam fading against the morning.

Below it, a woman in a weathered keeper’s coat walks the tide line.

Among dark stones: a shell glowing softly, as though holding the last light of a sunset.

She crouches. Holds her hand above it.

Warm gold touches her worn fingers.

She lifts it.

A single piano note, left to decay.

0:20–0:35 — INT. KEEPER’S COTTAGE — MORNING

She sets the shell beside a chipped cup.

Its glow washes across the table. Across her face.

A small smile almost forms.

MATCH CUT: The same composition, hours later. The cup untouched. The window black.

0:35–0:55 — INT. KEEPER’S COTTAGE — NIGHT

Rain streaks the glass.

The shell begins to pulse.

A low, unwavering tone enters the score.

Through the window, the lighthouse beam passes once.

Then darkness.

The keeper rises sharply.

Far offshore, two tiny ship lights approach the headland. Lightning reveals the black teeth of the reef below.

She looks from them to the shell.

Takes it in both hands.

0:55–1:20 — EXT./INT. LIGHTHOUSE — NIGHT

She crosses the yard, bent into the storm, sheltering the shell beneath her coat.

Inside: a narrow spiral staircase.

She climbs.

Fragments of movement: boots on wet stone; one hand gripping the rail; gold leaking between her fingers.

Through a slit window, the ship lights draw nearer.

She slips on a step. Catches herself.

For a moment, she presses the shell to her chest.

Then climbs again.

The strings gather urgency without losing their sorrow.

1:20–1:40 — INT. LANTERN ROOM / EXT. SEA — NIGHT

The great lens turns around a dead lamp.

She works the controls. Nothing.

She opens the lamp housing and places the shell inside.

A breath of darkness.

Then gold blossoms through the glass.

The beam sweeps across the storm—revealing the reef directly ahead of the nearest ship.

Its lights turn.

Beyond it, the second vessel changes course.

From high above: two small ships pass safely into open water.

The keeper rests her forehead against the lantern frame.

The score opens into a brief, aching warmth.

1:40–2:00 — EXT. BEACH — DAWN

Pale morning. The sea lies quiet.

The keeper walks back to the place where she found the shell. It is dim now, cupped in her hands.

She kneels and sets it among the stones.

Her fingers linger.

A thin wash of water reaches it. The glow strengthens faintly beneath the surface.

She withdraws her hand.

WIDE: The keeper walks toward the distant lighthouse, alone beneath an immense sky.

In the foreground, the shell waits at the water’s edge.

The opening piano note returns.

CUT TO BLACK.

Claude Opus 5.5

THE KEEPER'S SHELL

A short film. Approx. 2:00. No dialogue — score only.

Visual style: Painterly, like a moving oil painting. Visible brushstrokes, soft edges, muted blues and greys, with warm amber from the lamp and a cool pale-teal glow from the shell. The shell is the only thing that ever glows that colour. Score: Solo cello and piano at the core, with a soft choir/drone that enters with the shell. Melancholy, restrained, swelling only in the storm. Main character: THE KEEPER — a woman in her late 50s, weathered face, grey hair tied back, heavy navy wool sweater, long dark oilskin coat for the storm. Same look in every scene. Location: A lone white lighthouse with a red lamp room on a rocky point, a small stone keeper's cottage at its base, a grey-sand beach below.

SCENE 1 — DAWN ON THE BEACH (0:00–0:22)

1. Wide. Dawn. Pale pink and grey sky over a flat sea. The lighthouse stands on the point, its lamp turning slowly, fading against the brightening sky. Score: a single low cello note, held.

2. Medium. The Keeper walks along the waterline, coat pulled close, collecting driftwood into a canvas bag. Her footprints trail behind her.

3. Close. Something in the wet sand catches the light — a spiral shell, glowing a faint pale teal from inside.

4. Close on her face. She stops. Curiosity, a touch of wonder.

5. Insert. Her weathered hand lifts the shell from the sand. The glow pulses softly once, like a heartbeat. Score: a high, soft choir tone enters for the first time — the shell's theme.

SCENE 2 — BRINGING IT HOME (0:22–0:40)

6. Wide. She climbs the path up to the cottage, the shell held carefully in both hands.

7. Interior, cottage — medium. A small, sparse room: a wooden table, a kettle, a window facing the tower. She places the shell on the windowsill. Morning light. The glow is almost invisible in daylight.

8. Montage, soft dissolves. Her ordinary day: polishing the great lens in the lamp room, writing in the logbook, eating alone at the table. Each time, her eyes drift back to the shell on the sill. Score: piano, gentle and lonely.

SCENE 3 — NIGHT, THE HUM (0:40–1:00)

9. Interior, cottage — night. Darkness. The shell now glows clearly, teal light spilling across the sill and table. A visible ripple pulses through the glow, as if it is humming. Score: the choir tone returns, lower, with a vibrating drone underneath.

10. Close. The Keeper wakes in bed, turns toward the light.

11. Close. The water in a glass on the table trembles in rings.

12. Wide, exterior. The lighthouse against a darkening sky. The beam sweeps once… flickers… and goes out. Score: everything cuts to silence.

13. Medium. The Keeper at the window, staring at the dead tower. Wind rises. Rain begins to streak the glass. Far out at sea, the tiny lights of ships, pitching on the swell.

SCENE 4 — THE STORM CLIMB (1:00–1:35)

14. Medium. She pulls on her oilskin coat. Hesitates. Then takes the glowing shell from the sill. Score: low cello pulse, building.

15. Wide. She crosses from the cottage to the tower in the storm, bent into the wind, rain lashing, the shell glowing in her fist — the only light in the frame.

16. Interior, tower — low angle. The spiral staircase winding up into blackness. She climbs. The shell's teal light paints the curved walls as she rises.

17. Close. Her face — wet, determined, breathing hard.

18. Wide, exterior. Huge waves smash against the rocks. A ship's silhouette leans dangerously close to the point. Score: full strings and choir swell.

19. Interior, lamp room. Glass rattling, rain streaming down the windows. The great lens sits dark. She sets the shell into the heart of the lamp.

20. Extreme close. The shell's glow brightens… and brightens… passing into the lens.

21. Wide. The lamp blazes back to life — the beam now warm gold edged with teal — and sweeps out across the storm. Score: the shell's theme played at full strength.

22. Wide, sea. The beam crosses the water. The ship turns away from the rocks. Then another. Their lights move safely into open water.

SCENE 5 — MORNING AGAIN (1:35–2:00)

23. Wide. Dawn. The storm has passed. Calm sea, pale pink light — mirroring the opening shot. The lamp turns quietly. Score: solo piano, slow.

24. Interior, lamp room. The Keeper, exhausted, lifts the shell from the lamp. Its glow is softer now.

25. Medium. She walks down to the beach in the same place she found it.

26. Close. She kneels and places the shell back in the wet sand. She holds her hand over it for a moment. A small, sad smile.

27. Close. A gentle wave washes over the shell. When the water pulls back, the glow fades… and the shell is gone.

28. Wide, final. The Keeper stands alone at the waterline, the lighthouse behind her, as the sun rises fully. She turns and walks back toward the tower. Her footprints trail behind her, as in the first scene. Score: the shell's theme, once, very quietly, on solo cello. Fade to black.

TITLE CARD: The Keeper's Shell

Production notes
  • Continuity: Keep the Keeper's face, hair, sweater and coat identical across all scenes; generate a character reference image first and use it for every shot. Do the same for the shell (spiral shape, pale teal glow) and the lighthouse.
  • Colour logic: Teal = the shell / the magic. Amber = the lamp / human duty. In the relit beam, both colours combine.
  • Pacing: 28 shots in 2 minutes means an average of ~4 seconds per shot. Hold longer on the quiet moments (Scenes 1, 2 and 5) and cut faster in the storm (Scene 4).
  • Open question: Your outline doesn't say why the shell makes the lamp go out. I've left it ambiguous (the shell's hum "pulls" the light into itself, then gives it back). If you'd prefer a clearer cause, let me know.

2. Structured prompt, then script

GPT-6.1 Sol

Step 1 · Structured prompt

Video Script-Writing Prompt

Task

Write a complete, production-ready script for a two-minute, dialogue-free short film based on the requirements below. Use timed sequences, visual action, and musical direction rather than spoken narration.

Goal and Core Idea
  • Tell a clear visual story in which a lighthouse keeper discovers a mysterious glowing shell, uses it to restore the lighthouse during a storm and save ships, then returns it to the beach.
  • Convey melancholy, quiet courage, and the temporary nature of a gift.
  • Assumed thematic interpretation: Something borrowed from the sea helps protect others, then is returned. Suggest this through imagery without explaining the shell’s origin or powers.
Audience and Platform
  • Audience — assumed: General audiences who enjoy atmospheric, visually driven short films.
  • Platform — assumed: Online video or digital short-film presentation. No specific service was named.
Format and Technical Specifications
  • Format: Cinematic narrative short film.
  • Duration: Exactly 2 minutes, including any titles or credits if used.
  • Aspect ratio — assumed: 16:9 landscape.
  • Script format: Timestamped sequences totaling 120 seconds, with separate descriptions of visuals/action and score.
  • Dialogue: None.
  • Audio: Score only. No voice-over, spoken words, or standalone sound effects.
  • Titles/credits — assumed: Omit unless requested, preserving the full runtime for the story.
Tone and Visual Style
  • Melancholy, restrained, atmospheric, and painterly.
  • Assumed visual approach: Soft textures, expressive light, muted coastal colors, and luminous contrast around the shell and lighthouse lamp.
  • Let framing, movement, facial expression, and music carry the story.
  • Keep the successful rescue emotionally understated rather than triumphant.
  • Preserve the mystery; do not add explanatory lore.
User Requirements — Exact Quotations
  1. “A short film, 2 minutes.”
  2. “A lighthouse keeper finds a glowing shell on the beach at dawn.”
  3. “She brings it home.”
  4. “At night it hums and the lamp goes out.”
  5. “She climbs the tower in a storm and uses the shell to relight it.”
  6. “Ships are saved.”
  7. “Morning again, she puts the shell back on the beach.”
  8. “Melancholy, painterly, no dialogue, just score.”
Required Story Execution

Preserve the stated sequence:

  1. Dawn: the keeper discovers the glowing shell on the beach.
  2. She brings it home.
  3. Night: the shell hums and the lighthouse lamp goes out.
  4. During a storm, she climbs the tower with the shell.
  5. She uses the shell to relight the lamp.
  6. Clearly show that the restored lighthouse light saves ships.
  7. The following morning, she returns the shell to the beach.

Do not imply that the shell caused the outage unless necessary for the chosen interpretation; the user specified their timing, not a causal explanation.

Audio Interpretation

To reconcile “it hums” with “just score,” represent the shell’s humming through a sustained musical motif within the score, supported by visual cues. Do not introduce a separate diegetic humming effect, storm effects, footsteps, or ship sounds.

Uploads and Their Use
  • Uploads: “None.”
  • No uploaded footage, images, music, or reference material is available.
  • Create the script from the written request alone; do not refer to nonexistent assets.
Assumptions

All choices below are provisional, not user-stated requirements:

  • General audience; online or digital short-film presentation.
  • 16:9 landscape framing.
  • A timeless coastal setting, with no specified country or historical period.
  • An unnamed keeper; no added backstory is required.
  • Painterly cinematic imagery rather than a prescribed live-action or animation technique.
  • No explanatory on-screen text.
  • Musical representation of the shell’s hum to preserve score-only audio.
Missing Information Only the User Can Confirm
  • Intended audience and specific release platform.
  • Required aspect ratio, if different from 16:9.
  • Preferred production medium: live action, animation, or another technique.
  • Any mandatory branding, titles, credits, accessibility requirements, or production constraints.

Use the stated assumptions unless the user supplies these details; they do not prevent drafting.

Output Requirements

Return the script with:

  • Timestamped sequences totaling exactly 120 seconds.
  • Concrete, filmable visual action.
  • Score direction and transitions.
  • Clear visual causality between relighting the lighthouse and saving the ships.
  • No dialogue, voice-over, or added explanatory exposition.

Step 2 · Script

BORROWED LIGHT

Runtime: 02:00 exactly Frame: 16:9 Style: Painterly coastal imagery; slate blues, weathered grays, muted amber. Audio: Instrumental score only. All weather, machinery, footsteps, and other visible actions remain silent. No dialogue, voice-over, titles, or credits.

00:00–00:12 — DAWN / THE BEACH

Visuals / Action Wide shot: a lighthouse stands above a gray beach. Its beam fades against the coming daylight.

Below, the keeper walks along the tide line, shoulders tucked into an old coat.

A faint amber glow catches beneath a ribbon of seaweed. She stops, kneels, and lifts the seaweed aside.

Close-up: a small spiral shell lies in wet sand, illuminated from within. Its light touches her fingertips before she touches it.

Score Sparse, soft piano over a low bowed-string chord. As the shell appears, introduce a sustained viola harmonic: delicate, almost imperceptible.

00:12–00:24 — SHE BRINGS IT HOME

Visuals / Action She lifts the shell carefully. Water slips from its ridges.

Brief close-up of her face: curiosity without a smile.

Cut to her walking uphill toward the lighthouse, the shell sheltered between both hands.

Inside the keeper’s cottage at the tower’s base, she places it on a plain wooden table beside a cup and folded cloth. Its warm light rests against the cold daylight.

She removes her coat but remains looking at it.

Score The viola harmonic settles into a simple two-note motif. Piano notes remain widely spaced. Carry the final note across the transition.

00:24–00:38 — NIGHT / THE LIGHT FAILS

Visuals / Action Locked-off shot of the table. Dissolve from daylight to night: the window darkens; rain streaks the glass.

The shell’s glow gently swells and recedes. The keeper, seated nearby, raises her eyes toward it.

Outside, the lighthouse beam turns through storm clouds.

A lightning flash silhouettes the tower. The beam falters once, then disappears.

Back inside: the expected sweep of light no longer crosses the window. The shell continues its unchanged, gentle pulse.

The keeper stands immediately.

Score The shell motif becomes a sustained, softly wavering string tone—its “hum,” expressed entirely through music.

At the outage, remove the piano and lower strings, leaving the thin harmonic exposed. No impact or electrical sound.

00:38–00:50 — THE DANGER

Visuals / Action She tries the lighthouse control switch. Nothing.

Through the rain-blurred window, two sets of vessel lights approach the coast.

Cut to a high, wide view establishing the geography: two small fishing vessels are heading toward a low reef between them and the shore. Breaking water outlines the rocks only intermittently. The lighthouse remains dark above them.

The keeper looks from the dead controls to the shell.

She wraps it loosely in the cloth, leaving its luminous opening exposed, and takes it with her.

Score Low cellos enter with a restrained repeating pulse. The shell’s sustained motif remains above them, steady rather than urgent.

00:50–01:08 — THE CLIMB

Visuals / Action Looking down the tower’s spiral staircase: the keeper begins climbing, one hand gripping the rail, the other protecting the shell against her chest.

Closer shots: worn boots on narrow steps; her fingers tightening; amber light passing over peeling walls.

Rain drives through a damaged window. She turns her shoulder into it and continues.

Near the top, she pauses for one breath, visibly strained. Through the glass, the two vessels’ lights have drawn closer to the reef.

She pushes through the lantern-room door.

Score The cello pulse gains weight without accelerating. Add a rising, restrained string line. No percussion or heroic swell.

01:08–01:24 — RELIGHTING

Visuals / Action The enormous lighthouse lens is black.

She tries the lamp’s local control. Still nothing.

The shell’s reflection appears in the dark glass. She notices it.

Slowly, she unwraps the shell and brings it against the metal housing beneath the lamp.

Its amber light spreads across the housing, then gathers inside the bulb. The keeper shields her eyes but does not pull away.

The lamp catches.

Light fills the lens; the mechanism resumes its rotation. A beam cuts outward through the rain.

She lifts the shell away. The lighthouse stays lit. The shell now glows faintly in her hand.

Score The shell motif expands into a warm, sustained string chord. The cello pulse releases as the lamp catches.

Keep the musical arrival modest: relief, not victory.

01:24–01:43 — THE SHIPS ARE SAVED

Visuals / Action The restored beam sweeps across the reef, revealing jagged rocks directly ahead of the nearer vessel.

Inside its wheelhouse, a crew member sees the illuminated rocks through the windshield and turns the wheel hard.

Exterior: the vessel’s bow swings away. Its wake curves sharply around the reef’s outer edge.

The beam reaches the second vessel, farther offshore. Its course also changes; its running lights turn away from the coast.

Return to the same high, wide composition used before the climb: both vessels now pass safely on the seaward side of the exposed reef. Their widening wakes lead away from danger.

The lighthouse beam continues its measured sweep.

Score Two quiet harmonic changes accompany the vessels’ course corrections. The sustained strings soften as both ships clear the rocks.

No triumphant flourish. Let the tension simply loosen.

01:43–01:48 — AFTER

Visuals / Action In the lantern room, the keeper watches the departing vessel lights.

Her shoulders lower.

She looks down at the dim shell in her palm, then folds the cloth around it with care.

Score The piano returns with one low, gentle note. The shell motif is reduced to a fragile harmonic.

01:48–02:00 — MORNING / THE RETURN

Visuals / Action Cut to pale morning. The storm has passed.

The keeper kneels at the same patch of beach where she found the shell.

She unwraps it and places it in the shallow impression beneath the seaweed. Her fingertips linger, then withdraw.

Final low, wide shot: she walks away toward the lighthouse, small against the coast.

A thin wash of seawater reaches the shell. Its faint glow becomes difficult to distinguish from the reflected dawn.

Hold through 02:00. End.

Score A final, incomplete piano phrase. The viola harmonic thins and fades beneath it.

Let the last note decay naturally to silence at 02:00.

Claude Opus 5.5

Step 1 · Structured prompt

Video Script Brief: "The Shell" — Lighthouse Keeper Short Film

Goal and core idea

Write the script for a 2-minute wordless short film. A lone lighthouse keeper finds a glowing shell, brings it home, and when its strange power puts out the lighthouse lamp, she uses that same power in a storm to relight the lamp and save the ships. At dawn she returns the shell to the sea. The idea it must get across: something wondrous and borrowed — gratefully used, then let go. Themes of solitude, duty, quiet heroism, and the melancholy of returning what was never yours to keep.

Audience and platform
  • Audience: General adult audience that appreciates art-house / animated shorts and atmospheric storytelling.
  • Platform: Not specified. Assume online viewing (YouTube, Vimeo, film-festival-style screening), full-screen on desktop or TV.
Format, duration and aspect ratio
  • Format: Narrative short film, multiple scenes and shots, no dialogue, music score only.
  • Duration: 2 minutes (120 seconds), stated by user.
  • Aspect ratio: 16:9 widescreen (assumed; cinematic. 2.39:1 letterbox inside 16:9 is an acceptable stylistic choice).
Tone and style
  • Tone: Melancholy, quiet, contemplative, tender; tension during the storm; bittersweet resolution.
  • Visual style: Painterly — looks like a moving oil or gouache painting: visible brushstroke texture, soft edges, rich atmospheric colour. Consistent style across every shot.
  • Colour arc (suggested): cool pale pinks/blues at dawn → warm amber interior → deep blue-black night with the shell's glow → violent grey-green storm with the lamp's gold beam → soft, washed gold morning.
  • Pacing: Slow and lingering in the opening and ending; faster cutting in the storm climax.
  • Sound: Score only, plus optional natural sound (waves, wind, rain, the shell's hum). No dialogue, no voiceover, no on-screen text except optional title/credits.
  • The shell's glow and hum are the film's key visual/aural motif and must be consistent in colour and character every time they appear.
User requirements (quoted exactly)
  • "A short film, 2 minutes."
  • "A lighthouse keeper finds a glowing shell on the beach at dawn."
  • "She brings it home."
  • "At night it hums and the lamp goes out."
  • "She climbs the tower in a storm and uses the shell to relight it."
  • "Ships are saved."
  • "Morning again, she puts the shell back on the beach."
  • "Melancholy, painterly, no dialogue, just score."
Required story beats (in order, with suggested timing)
  1. Dawn, the beach (0:00–0:20): Establish the lighthouse, the sea, the keeper alone. She walks the shore and finds the glowing shell.
  2. Bringing it home (0:20–0:35): She carries it to the lighthouse cottage and places it somewhere meaningful; quiet domestic moments.
  3. Night, the hum (0:35–0:55): The shell hums and pulses. The lighthouse lamp flickers and goes out. Darkness over the sea.
  4. The storm and the climb (0:55–1:25): A storm rises; ships' lights are visible at sea in danger. She takes the shell and climbs the tower stairs through wind and rain.
  5. Relighting (1:25–1:40): At the top, she uses the shell to relight the lamp. The beam returns, sweeping the sea.
  6. Ships saved (1:40–1:50): The ships turn away from the rocks / reach safety.
  7. Morning, letting go (1:50–2:00): Calm morning. She returns the shell to the beach where she found it, and watches the tide; closing image.
Uploads and how they must be used
  • Uploads: None.
  • All characters, locations and designs must be created from scratch. Because there are no reference images, the script must define clear, repeatable descriptions of the keeper, the lighthouse, the cottage interior, and the shell so they stay consistent across all shots.
Assumptions (not stated by the user)
  • The keeper: A solitary woman, middle-aged to older, weathered, practical clothing (wool sweater, oilskin coat in the storm). Exact look is to be defined in the script and kept consistent.
  • Setting: A rocky, remote coast with a classic white (or white-and-red) stone lighthouse and an attached keeper's cottage; era is timeless/early 20th century, with an oil or early-electric lamp.
  • The shell: A spiral seashell, palm-sized, emitting a soft cool glow (e.g. pale blue-green or opal). How it "relights" the lamp is left poetic and visual (e.g. its light flows into the lamp), not explained.
  • Cause of the lamp failing: Implied, not explained — the shell's hum seems to draw the light away, creating the link that she must later reverse.
  • Ships: One or two sailing or fishing vessels at sea during the storm.
  • Animation/style: Painterly animated or painterly-rendered look, not photoreal live action.
  • Opening and closing shots mirror each other (beach at dawn / beach in morning) to frame the story.
  • Optional title card at the start or end; no credits text unless provided.

Step 2 · Script

THE SHELL

A wordless short film — 2:00

Format: Narrative short, no dialogue, score + natural sound Aspect ratio: 16:9 (optional 2.39:1 letterbox) Style: Painterly animation — a moving oil/gouache painting. Visible brushstrokes, soft edges, rich atmospheric colour, consistent in every shot.

Consistency bible

MARGARET (the keeper) — Woman in her late fifties. Weathered, wind-reddened face, grey-streaked dark hair pinned in a loose bun. Cream cable-knit wool sweater, dark grey wool skirt, brown leather boots. In the storm: a long mustard-yellow oilskin coat with hood. Moves slowly and deliberately; alone for a long time.

THE LIGHTHOUSE — White stone tower with a single red band below the lantern room, on a rocky headland. Black iron gallery rail. Brass lamp with a great glass Fresnel lens. Inside: a white spiral stone staircase with an iron handrail.

THE COTTAGE — Low stone cottage attached to the tower base. One room: small iron stove, wooden table, one chair, a windowsill facing the sea, an oil lamp.

THE SHELL — Palm-sized spiral seashell, pale ivory with an opal sheen. Glows a soft, cool blue-green. When it hums, the glow pulses slowly like breathing. The colour, pulse and hum never change across the film.

COLOUR ARC — Pale pink/blue dawn → warm amber interior → blue-black night (shell glow) → grey-green storm (gold beam) → washed gold morning.

1. EXT. BEACH BELOW THE LIGHTHOUSE — DAWN (0:00–0:20)

SHOT 1 (0:00–0:07) — Wide. Pale pink and blue sky. The lighthouse stands on the headland, its beam making a last slow sweep before it fades into daylight. Small waves fold onto grey sand. Score: A single solo cello note, long and low. Soft waves.

SHOT 2 (0:07–0:12) — Medium, tracking. MARGARET walks the waterline alone, hands in her sweater sleeves, head down. Her footprints trail behind her.

SHOT 3 (0:12–0:16) — Her POV. Among wet stones and kelp, something glows faintly blue-green beneath a thin wash of water.

SHOT 4 (0:16–0:20) — Close-up. She kneels and lifts THE SHELL. The glow lights her palm and the underside of her face. Her expression: wonder, very quiet. Score: A high, glassy tone joins the cello — the shell's motif.

2. INT. COTTAGE — DAY INTO EVENING (0:20–0:35)

SHOT 5 (0:20–0:25) — Wide. Margaret enters the amber-lit cottage, shell cupped in both hands, and closes the door on the wind.

SHOT 6 (0:25–0:30) — Medium. She places the shell on the windowsill, facing the sea, and adjusts it slightly — as if it should be able to see the water.

SHOT 7 (0:30–0:35) — Time passes in one held frame: she eats alone at the table; the light outside the window goes from gold to deep blue. The shell glows steadily on the sill. Score: Gentle piano joins, a simple, lonely melody.

3. INT./EXT. COTTAGE AND LIGHTHOUSE — NIGHT (0:35–0:55)

SHOT 8 (0:35–0:41) — Close-up. In the dark cottage, the shell begins to pulse — slowly, like breathing. A low hum rises. Margaret, asleep in the chair, stirs. Sound: The shell's hum, low and resonant.

SHOT 9 (0:41–0:46) — Wide exterior. The lighthouse beam sweeps the sea. On each pulse of the shell (seen as a faint blue-green glow in the cottage window below), the beam weakens.

SHOT 10 (0:46–0:51) — Close-up, lantern room. The lamp flickers. Thin threads of golden light drift out of it, as if drawn away. The flame dies.

SHOT 11 (0:51–0:55) — Wide. The lighthouse stands dark. Only the shell's small glow in the window remains, and beyond it, the black sea. Score: Music drops out. Only wind.

4. EXT. SEA / INT. LIGHTHOUSE STAIRS — STORM (0:55–1:25)

SHOT 12 (0:55–1:01) — Wide, sea. The storm breaks: grey-green waves, slanting rain. Two small fishing boats pitch toward the black rocks, their lanterns tiny and swinging. Score: Low strings swell; timpani rumble under the thunder.

SHOT 13 (1:01–1:05) — Interior. Margaret wakes, sees the dark tower through the window, then the ships' lights. She looks at the shell.

SHOT 14 (1:05–1:09) — Fast. She pulls on the mustard oilskin, grabs the shell, and opens the door into the storm. Rain blasts in.

SHOT 15 (1:09–1:15) — Low angle, spiral stairs. She climbs, one hand on the iron rail, the shell held against her chest. Its blue-green glow lights the curved white walls as she rises.

SHOT 16 (1:15–1:19) — Exterior. A wave crashes against the tower base; the whole frame shudders.

SHOT 17 (1:19–1:25) — Close-up. She stops halfway, breathing hard, and looks up the endless spiral. The shell pulses faster. She keeps climbing. Score: Building, quickening strings.

5. INT. LANTERN ROOM — STORM (1:25–1:40)

SHOT 18 (1:25–1:30) — Medium. She bursts into the lantern room. Rain lashes the glass. The great dark lens towers over her.

SHOT 19 (1:30–1:35) — Close-up. She holds the shell to the cold lamp. The shell's blue-green light flows out in thin threads into the lamp — the reverse of shot 10 — and slowly turns gold.

SHOT 20 (1:35–1:40) — Wide. The lamp blazes. The lens begins to turn. A gold beam cuts out across the storm. Score: Full orchestral swell, the shell's glassy motif inside it.

6. EXT. SEA — STORM (1:40–1:50)

SHOT 21 (1:40–1:45) — Wide. The gold beam sweeps over the two fishing boats. Their lanterns turn; the boats veer away from the rocks.

SHOT 22 (1:45–1:50) — Medium, lantern room. Margaret, soaked, watches through the streaming glass. In her hand, the shell's glow is now very faint. Score: Strings settle into a soft resolution.

7. EXT. BEACH — MORNING (1:50–2:00)

SHOT 23 (1:50–1:55) — Wide, mirrors shot 1. Calm, washed-gold morning. The sea is flat. Margaret walks down to the waterline, the shell in her palm, its glow barely there.

SHOT 24 (1:55–1:58) — Close-up. She kneels and sets the shell in the shallows where she found it. A small wave washes over it. For a moment, it glows blue-green again.

SHOT 25 (1:58–2:00) — Wide. She stands and watches the tide take the shell. The lighthouse stands behind her. Hold. Score: The solo cello returns with the single long note from the opening, then silence.

FADE OUT.

Open questions for you
  • Title: I've used The Shell as a working title. Do you want a title card or credits?
  • The keeper: Does my description of Margaret match what you picture (age, look, clothing)? Same for the lighthouse.
  • Painterly reference: Any painter, art movement or film to anchor the style?
  • The magic: I kept the shell–lamp link visual and unexplained (light drawn out, then flowed back). More explicit, or keep it ambiguous?
  • Score: I've used cello, piano and strings. Any instrumentation, composer reference or existing track you prefer?
  • Destination: Festival, YouTube or personal? This confirms aspect ratio and resolution.
  • Sound effects: Are natural sounds (waves, wind, rain, the shell's hum) OK, or score strictly only?

3. Story first, then operational chunks

GPT-6.1 Sol

Step 1 · Story

At dawn, the beach lies beneath a wash of pearl-grey light. Pools between the rocks hold the pale sky; ribbons of kelp mark the retreating tide. Above the shore stands a weathered lighthouse, its white walls stained by salt.

The keeper walks alone along the waterline, her coat hanging heavily from narrow shoulders. Among the broken shells, something glows.

She kneels. A small spiral shell rests in the sand, lit from within by a soft amber radiance. She turns it in her palm, and its light warms her weathered fingers. For a moment she remains there, reluctant to disturb it. Then she carries it home.

In the cottage beneath the tower, she places the shell beside a chipped cup on the kitchen table. Its glow persists through the fading day, a small warmth among worn wood, faded blue walls and neatly mended clothes. She eats alone, occasionally looking toward it.

At night, the shell begins to hum—a low, trembling thread within the musical score. Beyond the window, a storm gathers. Rain obscures the sea, and the lighthouse lamp goes out.

Far offshore, several ships drift toward the dark reef.

The keeper takes the shell and climbs the tower. Wind presses rain through the narrow windows. She grips the iron railing, ascending the worn steps with the shell held against her chest. Its light spills between her fingers.

At the top, she stands beside the extinguished lamp. She opens its glass housing and brings the shell close to the dark burner. Amber light gathers there, faint at first, then bright enough to illuminate her face. The lamp catches.

Its beam returns across the storm-dark water. The keeper watches it pass over the ships. One changes course, then another; their lights move steadily away from the reef toward open sea. She stays beside the lamp until they are safe.

Morning comes softly. The storm has left scattered weed and driftwood along the beach.

The keeper returns to the place where she found the shell. She kneels and settles it into the wet sand. Its glow is faint now, almost lost in the dawn.

Her hand lingers beside it before she stands. She walks back toward the lighthouse as a thin sheet of water reaches the shell. The score fades, and the returning tide leaves its spiral resting in the pale morning light.

Global requirements
  • Total duration: Exactly 120 seconds / 2 minutes. The chunk durations include all transitions. No additional title sequence, credits, or end card.
  • Aspect ratio: 16:9 landscape, a production default because the request does not specify a ratio. Maintain it across every chunk.

  • One female lighthouse keeper throughout; maintain the same face, narrow shoulders, weathered hands, tied-back grey hair, and heavy slate-grey coat.
  • One small spiral shell throughout; keep its shape, markings, scale, and internal amber light consistent. Its glow becomes faint on the final morning.
  • Maintain one lighthouse, attached coastal geography, cottage, lamp assembly, and reef.
  • Use the same three ships before and after the rescue; preserve their individual silhouettes and navigation lights.
  • Music and voice: No dialogue or narration; just score. Use a continuous melancholy instrumental score. Represent the shell’s hum as a low, trembling thread within that score, not as a separate sound-effects layer. No environmental Foley, voices, lyrics, or ship horns.
  • Style and mood: Melancholy and painterly throughout. Use textured, brushlike imagery, restrained performances, gentle camera movement, and unhurried editing. The rescue brings quiet relief, not a triumphant tonal shift.
  • Lighting and palette: Pearl-grey dawns; faded blues and worn browns inside the cottage; blue-black storm scenes; amber shell light as the recurring warm accent.
  • Editing continuity: Preserve screen direction, geography, object placement, and hand positions across dependent chunks. Use brief dissolves for substantial time changes; keep their overlap within the stated durations.
  • On-screen text: None throughout.
  • Source assets: No uploads were supplied. Generate the required visuals and instrumental score while maintaining consistent character, object, and environment references.

Claude Opus 5.5

Step 1 · Story

The Keeper and the Shell — a short film, about two minutes. No dialogue, just score.

Dawn comes in grey and rose over a cold northern shore. The sea is quiet, folding in long slow waves that hiss across dark sand. Above the beach, on a spur of black rock, stands the lighthouse — white paint weathered to the color of bone, its lamp room dim now that the night is over. Everything looks as though it were painted in oils: soft edges, heavy skies, light that seems to come from inside the clouds.

The keeper walks the tideline as she does every morning. She is in her fifties, wrapped in a long wool coat, a knitted scarf pulled up against the wind, grey hair loose at her temples. She moves slowly, carefully, a person used to being alone. The score begins with a single cello, low and patient.

Among the seaweed and broken driftwood, something glows. She stops. Half-buried in the wet sand lies a spiral shell, pale as pearl, and inside its curve there is a soft light — not reflected, but its own, a faint blue-gold pulse like a heartbeat. She kneels and lifts it. The glow warms her cupped hands. She looks out at the sea for a long moment, as if asking permission, and then she slips the shell into her coat pocket.

Inside the keeper's cottage at the foot of the tower, the morning is plain and small: a kettle, a single chair, a table with a logbook and a cracked cup. She sets the shell on the windowsill. Its light is fainter here, away from the water. She watches it while she drinks her tea, and the strings gather into a gentle, uncertain melody.

Night falls. Wind rises off the sea, and the first rain strikes the window. The great lamp turns above the cottage, its beam sweeping out across the black water. On the windowsill, the shell begins to hum — a low, resonant tone that blends into the score until it is hard to tell which is which. Its glow brightens and dims in long, slow waves. The keeper wakes in her chair and turns toward it.

Then the beam above her stutters. It flickers once, twice, and goes out. The tower stands dark against a sky now torn by storm. Far out on the water, small lights rock in the swell — ships, three of them, struggling toward a coast they can no longer see.

She does not hesitate. She takes the shell from the sill, pulls on her coat, and runs out into the rain. The storm is enormous: wind tearing at her scarf, waves exploding white against the rocks, lightning flaring the whole sea silver for an instant and then plunging it back into black. She pushes open the iron door of the tower and climbs. The spiral stairs wind up and up, slick with water, the shell glowing brighter in her hand with every turn, its hum rising with the music — drums now, deep and urgent, under the strings.

At the top, the lamp room is cold and silent. The great lens sits dead in its brass frame. She holds the shell up to it. For a breath, nothing. Then the shell's light pours out of its spiral and into the glass, flooding the lens with blue-gold fire. The lamp roars back to life. The beam sweeps out over the storm, broader and warmer than it has ever been, cutting a path of gold across the waves.

Out at sea, the ships turn. One by one, they find the beam and steer away from the rocks, their small lights sliding safely past the point and on toward the harbor. The keeper stands at the glass, soaked and shaking, watching until the last of them is clear. The score swells, then softens into a single sustained note.

Morning again. The storm has passed, and the sky is washed pale and clean. The sea lies calm. The keeper walks down to the beach, the shell in her hands. Its glow is very faint now, tired, as if it gave almost everything it had. She kneels at the same spot on the tideline where she found it and sets it gently in the wet sand. A small wave runs up and wraps around it, then pulls back.

She stays kneeling for a moment, then rises and walks back toward the lighthouse without looking back. Behind her, the shell's light pulses once, softly, and the next wave carries it away. The cello plays its first melody again, alone, and the painted dawn fades to white.

Step 2 · Operational chunks

The Keeper and the Shell: Operational Chunks

Recurring entities (referenced below):

  • Keeper: woman in her fifties, grey hair loose at the temples, long wool coat, knitted scarf. Same face, hair, coat and scarf colors in every chunk. Soaked in Chunks 7–10.
  • Shell: spiral shell, pale pearl color, with its own inner blue-gold light that pulses like a heartbeat. Same shape and size everywhere. Glow intensity changes by chunk: soft on the beach, fainter in the cottage, brightening at night, blazing in the lamp room, very faint and tired at the end.
  • Lighthouse: white tower weathered to bone color, on a spur of black rock above the beach. Lamp room with a great lens in a brass frame. Iron door at the base. Spiral stairs inside.
  • Cottage: small, at the foot of the tower. Kettle, a single chair, a table with a logbook and a cracked cup, a windowsill facing the sea.
  • Ships: three, seen only as small lights on the water.
  • Find spot: a specific spot on the tideline among seaweed and broken driftwood. The same spot is used in Chunks 3 and 11.

Chunk 1: Dawn shore

  • Duration: 8 s (0:00–0:08)
  • Visual: Wide establishing shot. Grey-rose dawn over a cold northern shore. Long slow waves hiss across dark sand. The lighthouse stands on black rock above the beach, its lamp room dim.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Waves, wind. No music yet, or a held breath of silence before the cello.
  • Entities: Lighthouse.
  • Environment: Northern beach, dawn. Grey and rose sky, heavy clouds lit from within. Painterly oil look, soft edges.
  • Depends on: None. Sets the style frame for the whole film.
  • Source: "Dawn comes in grey and rose over a cold northern shore. The sea is quiet, folding in long slow waves that hiss across dark sand. Above the beach, on a spur of black rock, stands the lighthouse — white paint weathered to the color of bone, its lamp room dim now that the night is over. Everything looks as though it were painted in oils: soft edges, heavy skies, light that seems to come from inside the clouds."

Chunk 2: The keeper walks

  • Duration: 8 s (0:08–0:16)
  • Visual: The keeper walks the tideline slowly, wrapped in her coat, scarf pulled up against the wind. She is small against the big sea.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Score begins: a single cello, low and patient. Waves and wind under it.
  • Entities: Keeper. Lighthouse (in background).
  • Environment: Same beach and dawn as Chunk 1.
  • Depends on: Chunk 1 (same beach, same light, same lighthouse).
  • Source: "The keeper walks the tideline as she does every morning. She is in her fifties, wrapped in a long wool coat, a knitted scarf pulled up against the wind, grey hair loose at her temples. She moves slowly, carefully, a person used to being alone. The score begins with a single cello, low and patient."

Chunk 3: The glowing shell

  • Duration: 12 s (0:16–0:28)
  • Visual: A glow among seaweed and driftwood. She stops. Close-up: the pearl spiral shell half-buried in wet sand, pulsing blue-gold. She kneels and lifts it, and the glow warms her cupped hands. She looks out at the sea for a long moment, then slips the shell into her coat pocket.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Cello continues. Soft wave wash.
  • Entities: Keeper. Shell (first appearance, establishes its design). Find spot.
  • Environment: Same beach and dawn. The shell's blue-gold is the only saturated color.
  • Depends on: Chunk 2 (same keeper, same light).
  • Source: "Among the seaweed and broken driftwood, something glows. She stops. Half-buried in the wet sand lies a spiral shell, pale as pearl, and inside its curve there is a soft light — not reflected, but its own, a faint blue-gold pulse like a heartbeat. She kneels and lifts it. The glow warms her cupped hands. She looks out at the sea for a long moment, as if asking permission, and then she slips the shell into her coat pocket."

Chunk 4: Morning in the cottage

  • Duration: 10 s (0:28–0:38)
  • Visual: Plain, small cottage interior: kettle, single chair, table with logbook and cracked cup. She sets the shell on the windowsill, where its glow is fainter. She drinks tea and watches it.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Strings gather into a gentle, uncertain melody. Kettle, cup on table.
  • Entities: Keeper (coat off or open, same scarf and hair). Shell (dimmer glow). Cottage.
  • Environment: Cottage interior, morning. Cool window daylight, muted palette.
  • Depends on: Chunk 3 (same shell, same keeper).
  • Source: "Inside the keeper's cottage at the foot of the tower, the morning is plain and small: a kettle, a single chair, a table with a logbook and a cracked cup. She sets the shell on the windowsill. Its light is fainter here, away from the water. She watches it while she drinks her tea, and the strings gather into a gentle, uncertain melody."

Chunk 5: Night, the shell hums

  • Duration: 10 s (0:38–0:48)
  • Visual: Night. Rain hits the window. The lamp beam sweeps over the black sea, its light passing over the cottage. On the sill, the shell's glow rises and falls in long slow waves. The keeper wakes in her chair and turns toward it.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Wind, first rain. The shell's low resonant hum blends into the score.
  • Entities: Keeper. Shell (brightening). Cottage. Lighthouse beam.
  • Environment: Cottage interior, night, storm starting. Dark blues, with the sweeping lamp beam and blue-gold shell glow.
  • Depends on: Chunk 4 (same cottage layout, same windowsill position of the shell).
  • Source: "Night falls. Wind rises off the sea, and the first rain strikes the window. The great lamp turns above the cottage, its beam sweeping out across the black water. On the windowsill, the shell begins to hum — a low, resonant tone that blends into the score until it is hard to tell which is which. Its glow brightens and dims in long, slow waves. The keeper wakes in her chair and turns toward it."

Chunk 6: The lamp goes out

  • Duration: 10 s (0:48–0:58)
  • Visual: The beam stutters, flickers twice and goes out. Exterior: the dark tower against a storm-torn sky. Far out on the water, three small ship lights rock in the swell.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Hum and score drop away to storm sound, then tension rises.
  • Entities: Lighthouse (dark). Ships (three lights).
  • Environment: Exterior, night, storm. Black and slate palette, no beam.
  • Depends on: Chunk 5 (same beam, same night).
  • Source: "Then the beam above her stutters. It flickers once, twice, and goes out. The tower stands dark against a sky now torn by storm. Far out on the water, small lights rock in the swell — ships, three of them, struggling toward a coast they can no longer see."

Chunk 7: Into the storm

  • Duration: 9 s (0:58–1:07)
  • Visual: She takes the shell from the sill, pulls on her coat and runs out into the rain. Wind tears at her scarf. Waves explode white on the rocks. Lightning flares the sea silver, then black. She pushes open the iron door of the tower.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Storm, thunder. Score builds.
  • Entities: Keeper (coat on, scarf, getting soaked). Shell (glowing in her hand). Lighthouse (iron door).
  • Environment: Exterior, night, full storm, lightning flashes.
  • Depends on: Chunk 5 (cottage), Chunk 6 (dark tower).
  • Source: "She does not hesitate. She takes the shell from the sill, pulls on her coat, and runs out into the rain. The storm is enormous: wind tearing at her scarf, waves exploding white against the rocks, lightning flaring the whole sea silver for an instant and then plunging it back into black. She pushes open the iron door of the tower…"

Chunk 8: The climb

  • Duration: 9 s (1:07–1:16)
  • Visual: Spiral stairs, wet and slick, winding up. She climbs. The shell glows brighter with every turn and lights the stairwell blue-gold.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Shell hum rises with the music. Drums enter, deep and urgent, under the strings.
  • Entities: Keeper (soaked). Shell (growing brighter).
  • Environment: Tower interior, night. Dark stone or iron, lit mainly by the shell.
  • Depends on: Chunk 7 (same keeper state, same shell).
  • Source: "…and climbs. The spiral stairs wind up and up, slick with water, the shell glowing brighter in her hand with every turn, its hum rising with the music — drums now, deep and urgent, under the strings."

Chunk 9: Relighting the lamp

  • Duration: 12 s (1:16–1:28)
  • Visual: The lamp room is cold and silent, the great lens dead in its brass frame. She holds up the shell. A beat of nothing. Then light pours from the shell's spiral into the lens and floods it with blue-gold fire. The lamp roars back. The beam sweeps out over the storm, broader and warmer than before, cutting a gold path across the waves.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Near-silence for the held breath, then the score surges.
  • Entities: Keeper. Shell (blazing, then spent). Lighthouse lens and brass frame. The new beam is blue-gold and warmer than the old beam.
  • Environment: Lamp room, night, storm outside the glass. Shifts from dark to blazing blue-gold.
  • Depends on: Chunk 8 (same keeper and shell). The beam must look like Chunk 5's beam, but warmer and broader.
  • Source: "At the top, the lamp room is cold and silent. The great lens sits dead in its brass frame. She holds the shell up to it. For a breath, nothing. Then the shell's light pours out of its spiral and into the glass, flooding the lens with blue-gold fire. The lamp roars back to life. The beam sweeps out over the storm, broader and warmer than it has ever been, cutting a path of gold across the waves."

Chunk 10: Ships saved

  • Duration: 10 s (1:28–1:38)
  • Visual: At sea, the three ships turn into the beam one by one and slide safely past the point toward the harbor. Intercut with the keeper at the glass, soaked and shaking, watching until the last ship is clear.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Score swells, then softens to a single sustained note.
  • Entities: Ships (three lights). Keeper. Lighthouse beam.
  • Environment: Sea at night, storm easing. Gold beam path on dark water.
  • Depends on: Chunk 6 (same three ships), Chunk 9 (same beam).
  • Source: "Out at sea, the ships turn. One by one, they find the beam and steer away from the rocks, their small lights sliding safely past the point and on toward the harbor. The keeper stands at the glass, soaked and shaking, watching until the last of them is clear. The score swells, then softens into a single sustained note."

Chunk 11: Returning the shell

  • Duration: 10 s (1:38–1:48)
  • Visual: Morning. Pale, clean sky. Calm sea. She walks down to the beach with the shell, its glow very faint and tired. She kneels at the find spot and sets it in the wet sand. A small wave wraps around it and pulls back.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: Gentle waves. The score is quiet and sparse.
  • Entities: Keeper (dry coat and scarf). Shell (very faint). Find spot.
  • Environment: Same beach as Chunks 1–3, morning after the storm. Washed pale palette.
  • Depends on: Chunk 3 (exact same find spot and framing echo). Chunks 1–2 (same beach).
  • Source: "Morning again. The storm has passed, and the sky is washed pale and clean. The sea lies calm. The keeper walks down to the beach, the shell in her hands. Its glow is very faint now, tired, as if it gave almost everything it had. She kneels at the same spot on the tideline where she found it and sets it gently in the wet sand. A small wave runs up and wraps around it, then pulls back."

Chunk 12: Letting go

  • Duration: 12 s (1:48–2:00)
  • Visual: She stays kneeling, then rises and walks back toward the lighthouse without looking back. Behind her, the shell pulses once, softly, and the next wave carries it away. The painted dawn fades to white.
  • On-screen text: None
  • Audio: The cello plays its first melody again, alone. It ends with the fade.
  • Entities: Keeper. Shell (final pulse). Lighthouse.
  • Environment: Same beach, pale dawn. Fade to white.
  • Depends on: Chunk 11 (continuous action). Chunk 2 (the cello melody repeats).
  • Source: "She stays kneeling for a moment, then rises and walks back toward the lighthouse without looking back. Behind her, the shell's light pulses once, softly, and the next wave carries it away. The cello plays its first melody again, alone, and the painted dawn fades to white."
Global requirements
  • Total duration: 2 minutes (120 s). The chunk durations above sum to 120 s.
  • No dialogue: no spoken words, no narration, no voice of any kind. Just score plus natural sound (sea, wind, rain, thunder, the shell's hum blended into the music).
  • Score: one continuous original score. A solo cello theme opens the film (Chunk 2) and returns alone at the end (Chunk 12). Strings gather in the cottage. Drums enter for the climb. The score swells at the relighting and resolves to a single sustained note.
  • Tone: melancholy throughout, even in the triumphant beats.
  • Style: painterly, oil-painting look in every chunk: soft edges, heavy skies, light that seems to come from inside the clouds. Same grade and texture across all chunks.
  • Identity: the keeper, shell, lighthouse, cottage and three ships stay consistent throughout (see recurring entities). Generate a character sheet for the keeper and a reference image for the shell first, and use them in every chunk.
  • On-screen text: none (no title card was requested).
  • Aspect ratio: not specified. Recommend 16:9, or 2.39:1 for a cinematic short-film feel.
  • Light arc: dawn, then morning, then night storm, then morning again. The blue-gold of the shell is the main color accent throughout.

4. Director's shooting script

GPT-6.1 Sol

BORROWED LIGHT

Director’s shooting script — 120 seconds

Visual approach: Painterly compositions; soft grain; restrained camera movement. Dawn is pearl-gray and faded rose. Night is indigo and charcoal. The shell gives off warm, opalescent light—the film’s only saturated color.

Audio approach: Score only: felt piano, low strings, and bowed-glass harmonics. No dialogue, narration, or diegetic sound effects. The shell’s “hum” is expressed through a sustained musical tone synchronized with its glow.

On-screen text: None throughout. The title above is not shown in the film.

All transitions are included in the timings below.

Claude Opus 5.5

THE KEEPER'S SHELL — Shooting Script

Runtime: 2:00 (120 seconds) Format: 2.39:1 widescreen Style: Painterly. Every frame should feel like a moving oil painting: visible brush texture, soft edges, rich impasto highlights on water and light. Palette moves from pale rose and grey-blue (dawn) to warm amber (home) to deep indigo and slate (night/storm) and back to soft gold (morning). Dialogue: None. On-screen text: Title and end card only. Score: Solo cello and piano, with a low string pad. One recurring motif, "the shell theme": a simple four-note rising phrase on celesta or glass harmonica. It belongs to the shell and appears whenever the shell glows.

Character: THE KEEPER, a woman in her sixties, weathered face, grey hair tied back, heavy wool sweater, oilskin coat, rubber boots. Same look in every shot. The shell: A palm-sized spiral shell, pale pearl-white, that glows from within with a soft blue-green light. Same shape and glow color in every shot. The lighthouse: White tower with a single faded red band, on a rocky point. A small stone cottage sits at its base.

SCENE 1 — EXT. BEACH BELOW THE LIGHTHOUSE — DAWN (0:00 – 0:22)

Shot 1 — Extreme wide, static | 0:00 – 0:07

  • Action: Pale dawn over a grey sea. The lighthouse stands on the point, its lamp turning one last slow sweep before it switches off for the day. Tiny in the frame, the Keeper walks along the tideline.
  • Camera: Locked-off.
  • On-screen text: Title fades in at 0:02, out at 0:06: THE KEEPER'S SHELL
  • Sound: Soft waves, distant gulls, wind. Score: a single sustained cello note enters.

Shot 2 — Medium, slow tracking (left to right) | 0:07 – 0:12

  • Action: The Keeper walks, head down, scanning the sand. She stops.
  • Camera: Slow lateral track alongside her, then stops when she stops.
  • Sound: Boots in wet sand. Piano joins the cello, slow and sparse.

Shot 3 — Close-up, static, low angle at sand level | 0:12 – 0:16

  • Action: Among seaweed and pebbles, the spiral shell pulses with a faint blue-green glow as a wave retreats from around it.
  • Camera: Static, lens almost touching the sand.
  • Sound: Hiss of receding water. Shell theme plays for the first time, quietly, on celesta.

Shot 4 — Close-up, slow push-in | 0:16 – 0:22

  • Action: Her weathered hands lift the shell. The glow lights her fingers and then her face as she raises it. Her expression: curiosity, a trace of loneliness.
  • Camera: Slow push-in from hands to face.
  • Sound: Waves soften. Cello holds under the shell theme.
SCENE 2 — INT. KEEPER'S COTTAGE — DAY INTO EVENING (0:22 – 0:38)

Shot 5 — Medium wide, static | 0:22 – 0:28

  • Action: A small, tidy cottage room: a kettle, a single chair, a shelf of old logbooks. She places the shell on the windowsill. Through the window, the lighthouse tower.
  • Camera: Locked-off.
  • Sound: Door closing, kettle beginning to warm, clock ticking. Piano, gentle.

Shot 6 — Time-lapse, wide, static | 0:28 – 0:34

  • Action: Light moves across the room as the day passes. She sits, drinks tea, writes in her log, glancing at the shell. Outside, the sky goes from gold to dusk violet. The lighthouse lamp switches on.
  • Camera: Locked-off, same framing as Shot 5.
  • Sound: Ambient sounds compressed and blurred. Score: piano figure repeats, slowly building.

Shot 7 — Close-up, static | 0:34 – 0:38

  • Action: The shell on the sill, glowing softly in the dark room, the lighthouse beam passing behind it through the glass.
  • Camera: Static.
  • Sound: Silence except the clock. Shell theme, very soft.
SCENE 3 — INT./EXT. COTTAGE AND LIGHTHOUSE — NIGHT (0:38 – 0:56)

Shot 8 — Medium, static | 0:38 – 0:43

  • Action: The Keeper asleep in her chair. The shell begins to hum. Its glow pulses, stronger.
  • Camera: Static.
  • Sound: A low, resonant hum rises, more felt than heard. Score drops away.

Shot 9 — Extreme wide, static | 0:43 – 0:47

  • Action: The lighthouse beam sweeps across black water. Then it flickers and dies. Darkness.
  • Camera: Locked-off.
  • Sound: The hum peaks, then cuts to silence at the moment the lamp dies.

Shot 10 — Close-up, static | 0:47 – 0:51

  • Action: Her eyes open. She looks to the window. No beam passes.
  • Camera: Static.
  • Sound: Wind rising outside. First rumble of distant thunder.

Shot 11 — Wide, slow push-in through the window | 0:51 – 0:56

  • Action: Out at sea, the storm is arriving. Tiny lights of ships on the horizon, rolling in heavy swell. She stands, takes the shell, pulls on her oilskin.
  • Camera: Slow push-in past her silhouette toward the window.
  • Sound: Low strings enter, tense and sustained. Rain begins against the glass.
SCENE 4 — EXT./INT. LIGHTHOUSE — STORM (0:56 – 1:24)

Shot 12 — Wide, handheld | 0:56 – 1:01

  • Action: She crosses the rocks from cottage to tower in driving rain, coat whipping, the glowing shell clutched to her chest. Waves explode against the rocks behind her.
  • Camera: Handheld, buffeted, following.
  • Sound: Full storm: wind howl, rain, crashing surf. Cello begins a driving ostinato.

Shot 13 — Low angle, static | 1:01 – 1:04

  • Action: She hauls the heavy tower door open against the wind and disappears inside.
  • Camera: Static, looking up the tower.
  • Sound: Heavy iron door groan and slam.

Shot 14 — Overhead, looking straight down the spiral stair, static | 1:04 – 1:10

  • Action: The spiral staircase coils down into darkness. The only light is the shell's blue-green glow, rising as she climbs, step after step.
  • Camera: Locked-off top-down.
  • Sound: Boots on iron steps, her breathing, wind moaning through the tower. Ostinato builds.

Shot 15 — Medium close-up, tracking up the stairs | 1:10 – 1:16

  • Action: She climbs, exhausted, gripping the rail. She slips, catches herself, keeps going. The shell's glow grows brighter with each turn.
  • Camera: Tracks up the stairwell just ahead of her.
  • Sound: Thunder shakes the tower. Strings swell. Shell theme threads through the ostinato.

Shot 16 — Wide, static | 1:16 – 1:24

  • Action: The lamp room. Rain lashes the glass on all sides. The great lens is dark. Through the glass, a ship's lights pitch dangerously close to the rocks. She stands before the lens, holding the shell.
  • Camera: Locked-off, wide enough to show the lens, her, and the ship beyond.
  • Sound: Storm at full force. Score holds one tense, unresolved chord.
SCENE 5 — INT./EXT. LAMP ROOM AND SEA — STORM (1:24 – 1:40)

Shot 17 — Close-up, slow push-in | 1:24 – 1:28

  • Action: She places the shell into the heart of the lens. A beat of nothing.
  • Camera: Slow push-in on the shell.
  • Sound: Score drops out. Only the storm, then a held silence.

Shot 18 — Extreme close-up, static | 1:28 – 1:31

  • Action: The shell blazes. Blue-green light floods through the prisms of the lens and turns warm white.
  • Camera: Static.
  • Sound: The hum returns, this time rising into a bright, singing tone. Shell theme plays in full, on strings and celesta together.

Shot 19 — Extreme wide, static | 1:31 – 1:36

  • Action: From the sea: the lighthouse beam blazes back to life and sweeps across the storm. The ship near the rocks turns away just in time. Further out, two more ships correct course.
  • Camera: Locked-off.
  • Sound: Full orchestral swell, the emotional peak of the score. A distant ship's horn sounds, low and grateful.

Shot 20 — Close-up, static | 1:36 – 1:40

  • Action: The Keeper, lit by the turning beam, watches the ships' lights move to safety. Rain on her face. Relief, and a quiet sadness.
  • Camera: Static.
  • Sound: The swell settles into cello alone. Storm begins to ease.
SCENE 6 — EXT. BEACH BELOW THE LIGHTHOUSE — MORNING (1:40 – 2:00)

Shot 21 — Extreme wide, static | 1:40 – 1:45

  • Action: Calm morning. Soft gold light over a quiet sea. The storm is gone. The lighthouse lamp turns its last sweep of the night and switches off, as in Shot 1.
  • Camera: Locked-off, mirroring Shot 1's framing.
  • Sound: Gentle waves, gulls. Piano returns, slow.

Shot 22 — Medium, slow tracking (right to left, reversing Shot 2) | 1:45 – 1:50

  • Action: She walks back along the tideline, holding the shell. Its glow is dimmer now, softly pulsing.
  • Camera: Slow lateral track, opposite direction to Shot 2.
  • Sound: Boots in wet sand. Cello and piano together.

Shot 23 — Close-up, low angle at sand level, static (mirrors Shot 3) | 1:50 – 1:54

  • Action: She kneels and sets the shell back on the sand where she found it. Her hand rests on it for a moment, then lifts away. A wave washes around it.
  • Camera: Static, same angle as Shot 3.
  • Sound: Hiss of the wave. Shell theme, one last time, slower, unresolved.

Shot 24 — Extreme wide, slow pull-back | 1:54 – 2:00

  • Action: She walks back toward the lighthouse, small against the sea. Behind her, the shell's glow fades into the sand as the tide takes it. Fade to black at 1:58.
  • Camera: Slow pull-back and rise.
  • On-screen text: None (or end credits after 2:00 if needed)
  • Sound: Cello holds a final low note into silence with the waves. Last sound: a single wave at 1:59.
Continuity notes
  • Keeper's face, hair, clothes, and age stay identical in every shot.
  • The shell keeps the same shape, size, and blue-green glow throughout. Its glow intensity tells the story: faint (found), soft (home), strong (hum), blazing (relight), dim (return).
  • Shots 1/21, 2/22, and 3/23 are mirrored pairs. Match framing, lens, and composition so the ending clearly echoes the opening.
  • The painterly texture stays consistent across day, night, and storm.

Baseline — full state at the first frame

Runtime: 120 seconds, exactly 2 minutes. Aspect ratio: 16:9. Style: Melancholy, painterly short film; textured surfaces, softly blended light, restrained movement and naturalistic proportions. No dialogue, captions, titles or logos. Palette: Pearl grey, slate blue, faded blue, weathered white and muted brown, with the shell’s amber glow as the principal warm accent. Audio: A restrained, melancholy instrumental score is already playing. No dialogue or separate environmental sound effects. The shell’s later hum will be represented within the score.

Characters and persistent objects
  • Keeper: One woman, alone; narrow shoulders, weathered hands, subdued expression. For visual continuity: greying hair tied back, a heavy charcoal coat, muted practical clothing with neat repairs, and dark boots. She starts standing just outside the opening composition, beside the waterline. Her hands are empty.
  • Shell: A small spiral shell resting among broken shells in beach sand. It already emits a soft internal amber glow, but is not distinguishable in the opening wide shot.
  • Lighthouse: Above the shore; weathered white walls stained by salt. Its tower contains worn stairs, an iron railing, narrow windows and a lamp with a glass housing and burner. The lamp is operating; its housing is closed.
  • Cottage: Beneath the tower. Worn wooden kitchen table, faded blue walls, neatly mended clothes, a chipped cup, and a simple meal with an eating utensil. These are initially off-screen.
  • Ships: Several vessels far offshore, initially travelling steadily in open water. Their navigation lights are on.
  • Reef: A fixed dark reef offshore.
  • Products: None.
Environment, time and lighting
  • Dawn on a rocky beach during a retreating tide.
  • Pools between the rocks reflect the pale sky; ribbons of kelp and broken shells lie along the shore.
  • No storm or rain yet. The sea is relatively calm.
  • Diffuse pearl-grey daylight illuminates the beach. Cottage interiors receive subdued window light; the operating lighthouse lamp supplies its own light.
  • No fresh storm-cast driftwood is present yet.

Opening composition: A wide view of beach, reflective rock pools and kelp, with the lighthouse above the shore. The keeper, cottage interior, individual shell and ships are not yet distinguishable.

Inheritance rule: Each chunk starts from the end state of its dependency. A cut changes the view, not the existence or condition of objects left off-screen. Everything not listed under Changes remains unchanged.

The Keeper and the Shell — Operational Chunks

Baseline (first frame)
  • Character — The keeper (locked): woman in her fifties, grey hair loose at her temples, long wool coat, knitted scarf. Slow, careful movement. No dialogue at any point.
  • Object — The shell (locked when introduced): spiral shell, pale as pearl, with its own soft blue-gold light pulsing like a heartbeat inside its curve.
  • Object — The lighthouse (locked): on a spur of black rock above the beach, white paint weathered to the color of bone, lamp room at the top with a great lens in a brass frame, iron door at the base, spiral stairs inside.
  • Location — The cottage (locked when introduced): at the foot of the tower; kettle, single chair, table with a logbook and a cracked cup, windowsill.
  • Environment: cold northern shore, dark sand, quiet sea with long slow waves, seaweed and broken driftwood on the tideline.
  • Time of day: dawn.
  • Lighting: grey and rose dawn light, light that seems to come from inside the clouds; lighthouse lamp dim.
  • Palette: greys, rose, bone white, dark sand, muted sea blues.
  • Style: painterly, like an oil painting — soft edges, heavy skies. Melancholy.
  • Audio: score only, no dialogue. Ambient sea (waves hissing on sand). Score not yet started.
  • Aspect ratio: not specified in the request — 2.39:1 or 16:9 widescreen (16:9 default).
  • Total duration: 2 minutes (120 s).
Chunks

Chunk 1 — Dawn shore

  • Quote: "Dawn comes in grey and rose over a cold northern shore. The sea is quiet, folding in long slow waves that hiss across dark sand. Above the beach, on a spur of black rock, stands the lighthouse — white paint weathered to the color of bone, its lamp room dim now that the night is over. Everything looks as though it were painted in oils: soft edges, heavy skies, light that seems to come from inside the clouds."

“make something cool for my insta”

1. One-shot script

Instagram Reel: “Nothing Special”

Length: 15 seconds · Style: cinematic, quick cuts, playful twist

0:00–0:03 — The boring opening Sit on your couch or stand in your room. Keep the shot still and ordinary. Voiceover: “Nothing special happened today.” On-screen text: just a normal day

0:03–0:04 — The switch Cover the lens with your hand. Cut to your hand pulling away somewhere new. Audio: The beat drops as the lens clears.

0:04–0:10 — Make ordinary look expensive Quick close-ups: shoes hitting the pavement, pouring a drink, grabbing your keys, sunlight on your face. Mix one slow-motion shot with fast cuts. Voiceover: “So I decided to film it like something did.”

0:10–0:13 — The hero shot Walk past the camera or look back over your shoulder. Hold the shot for a beat. On-screen text: no occasion. just a vibe.

0:13–0:15 — The punchline Hard cut to you back on the couch, eating a snack. Let the music stop. Voiceover: “Anyway. Back to doing nothing.”

Caption: “Low-budget day. High-budget energy.”

Step 2 · Script

Provisional concept: “Ordinary, Reframed”

An ordinary glass of water becomes cinematic through light, framing, and movement.

Assumptions—not confirmed preferences: This is a 15-second, vertical 9:16 Instagram Reel. “Cool” is interpreted as clean, cinematic, and visually polished. A drinking glass is the proposed subject, not something you requested. No promotional message or call to action is assumed.

What to film

Suggested setup: A phone, a clear drinking glass, water, and a table beside a window. Use a desk lamp from the side if daylight is unavailable. No existing footage is required.

Timed script
TimeShot-by-shot visualsExact on-screen text / optional voiceoverEditing and audio cues
0:00–0:02Start with a hand covering the lens. Pull it away quickly to reveal an empty glass on the table, filmed from a plain, eye-level angle.“Nothing special?”Reveal within the first half-second. Begin with a soft whoosh and a single musical beat.
0:02–0:04Cut to a tight shot of the glass rim catching the light. Slowly move the phone a few centimeters sideways so the highlight travels across the rim.No text.Hard cut on the beat. Keep the movement controlled; avoid digital zoom.
0:04–0:07Pour water into the glass. Frame tightly enough that the stream and rising water fill most of the screen.No text.Let the pouring sound come forward. Optional slow motion if the phone supports it.
0:07–0:10Film slightly above the glass as the water ripples settle. Position the light to catch the surface.No text.Cut from the falling stream to the moving ripples. Add a subtle musical build.
0:10–0:13.5Visual payoff: Reveal the filled glass from a low, three-quarter angle, with light shining through it and its shadow visible on the table. Make a small, steady push toward it.“Look closer.” Optional VO: “Same glass. Different perspective.”Land the reveal on the strongest beat. Hold long enough for the composition to register.
0:13.5–0:15A hand approaches and fully covers the lens. End on the covered frame.No text.Fade text before the hand enters. Match the final covered frame to the opening so replay feels like another reveal.
Production notes
  • Shoot the ordinary wide shot first, then move closer and lower for the cinematic shots.
  • Lock focus and exposure for each shot; lower exposure slightly if the glass highlights look blown out.
  • Use clean cuts, one consistent color treatment, and small white text placed away from Instagram’s interface.
  • Audio is an enhancement: choose a restrained instrumental with a beat change near 10 seconds. If using music, check usage rights for the intended account and post.
  • The concept works without sound: ordinary view → striking details → polished reveal. No user-specific wording or branding is needed for this draft.

Step 2 · Script

Script: "Infinite Window" — Instagram Reel

Concept: One seamless infinite zoom. We start on a neon city at night, dive into a single lit window, through a tiny world inside it, out into space — and land back on the same city. It loops perfectly, so people watch it two or three times before they realise it's restarted.

Format: Single continuous zoom (built from linked segments with invisible transitions), designed to loop Duration: 12 seconds Aspect ratio: 9:16 vertical, 1080×1920. All key action dead-centre so the 4:5 feed preview and 1:1 grid crop still work. Audio: Royalty-free upbeat electronic track (~120 BPM) with a riser and drop. Swappable for a trending Instagram sound. No dialogue, no voiceover. Works fully with the sound off. On-screen text: None.

Beat-by-beat
TimeVisualSound
0:00–0:01 — HookAlready moving: a rain-soaked neon city at night, seen from above. Pink and cyan signs glow (no readable words), reflections shimmer on wet streets. The camera is already zooming forward fast toward one tall tower.Music already playing mid-phrase.
0:01–0:03The zoom locks onto a single warm-yellow window high on the tower, the only lit one on its floor. Rain streaks past the lens.Riser begins.
0:03–0:05We pass through the glass. Inside: a cosy dark room, and on the desk, a glowing snow globe. The camera doesn't stop — it dives straight into the globe.Glass "shimmer" whoosh.
0:05–0:07Inside the globe: a tiny snowy forest under the northern lights. Green and violet aurora ripples overhead. The camera rushes up into the aurora.Riser peaks.
0:07–0:09The aurora opens into deep space: a field of stars, then a slowly turning planet streaked with pink and cyan light.Drop. Bass hits.
0:09–0:11The camera dives toward the planet's night side. The pink and cyan lights resolve into city grids — and then into the same rain-soaked neon city from the opening.Beat continues.
0:11–0:12 — Loop pointThe final frame matches the first frame exactly: same city, same angle, same zoom speed, rain mid-fall.Music cut on the downbeat so the restart hits on beat.
Generation prompts (per segment)

Each segment's last frame becomes the next segment's first frame. Constant forward zoom speed throughout; no cuts.

  1. Aerial view of a rain-soaked neon city at night, pink and cyan glow, wet streets reflecting light, camera zooming fast toward one tall tower, then onto a single warm-yellow lit window. No readable text or logos. Cinematic, high contrast, 9:16.
  2. Camera passes through a rainy window into a cosy dark room and dives straight into a glowing snow globe on a desk. Continuous forward motion.
  3. Inside the snow globe: a tiny snowy forest under bright green and violet northern lights; camera rushes upward into the aurora, which opens into a deep field of stars.
  4. A slowly turning planet in space streaked with pink and cyan lights; camera dives toward the night side as the lights resolve into a rain-soaked neon city seen from above, matching the opening frame exactly.

Avoid: real people, real brands, logos, readable signs or text, copyrighted characters, visible cuts or jumps in zoom speed.

Loop check: Play the last 0.5 s into the first 0.5 s. The motion must feel continuous with no jump in colour, angle or speed.
